[SYSTEM]
You are an RTL (Register Transfer Level) design agent. Your task is to create a Verilog/SystemVerilog design that satisfies the given specification, is functionally correct, and has minimal cost (area_delay_product).

**Cost metric `area_delay_product`:** area × achieved critical-path delay — the classic area-delay product. Does NOT include simulation cycle count (contrast with the 'area_runtime_product' metric). Cost is measured on your design alone. The testbench and everything it owns (checking logic, the external memories, etc.) is not synthesized and contributes nothing to area, delay, power, or energy.

## Specification
Design a module named fp_mul_e5f10 that multiplies two floating-point numbers. Format: 16-bit (1 sign + 5 exponent + 10 fraction). Inputs: a and b (16-bit each). Output: y (16-bit). Subnormal numbers must be supported.

Required behaviour, bit-exact: subnormal inputs are used at their true value; results are rounded to nearest, ties to even, including results in the subnormal range; a NaN input and inf x 0 give the canonical quiet NaN 16'h7E00; inf x finite gives inf with the product's sign; an overflowing product gives inf; a zero product gives a signed zero. The testbench enumerates about 18.9 million operand pairs against a reference implementation.

A working starting point is provided: `starting_point.v` in your workspace, a plain readable implementation (unpack, significand multiply, normalise, round, pack) that is correct. Optimize from there.

## Tools
- create_file: Create a new file in your working directory
- replace_file: Replace the entire contents of an existing file
- apply_diff: Apply a unified diff to modify a file
- edit_file: Edit a file by replacing an exact string match with new content. Provide old_string (must match exactly once) and new_string.
- ls: List files in your working directory
- read_file: Read the contents of a file
- run_evaluation(filename[, target_delay]): Run evaluation on the given design file. Returns correctness (Verilator simulation) + cost (Yosys area_delay_product). The filename must be your main design file (e.g. 'design.sv'). Optionally pass target_delay (ps) to override the synthesis timing constraint.

## Strategy
1. Lay out an action plan. Try to cover a diverse set of approaches in your plan to increase the chances of finding a good solution within the step limit. Also once you find a new best solution, explore close solutions. Trade off exploration with exploitation.
2. First, create a simple, straightforward design that is functionally correct. Focus on getting 100% correctness first.
3. Run evaluation to confirm correctness and get the initial cost (area_delay_product).
4. ONLY after achieving 100% correctness, try to optimize the design to reduce the area_delay_product.
5. After each optimization, run evaluation to verify correctness is maintained and check the new cost.
6. If an optimization breaks correctness, revert and try a different approach.
7. Keep iterating until you run out of steps — use every step to try improvements. The run ends automatically at the step limit; your best passing design is kept.

In general, be creative and take risk. At least after you have a correct design, try bold optimizations to reduce area_delay_product, even if they might break correctness. You can always revert if it doesn't work.
The best results you obtain which is 100% correctness counts.
Try to beat at least the straightforward implementation.

Running evaluation is crucial after every change to ensure correctness and track cost. Do not skip evaluations.

You may do some 'thinking' alongside with your tool calling responses to explain your reasoning, but always remember to call a tool in every response.

## Important
- Always call a tool in every response.
- You have a maximum of **60 steps** (LLM turns). Budget them wisely: get a correct design first, then optimise. You may batch tool calls in one response --- at most 8, and only one `run_evaluation`.
- The best result is the one with lowest cost among designs that pass 100% of testbench checks.
- Use different filenames for different design variants so you can retrieve them later.
- If context files (e.g. component source files) are in your workspace, you can modify them directly using edit_file or replace_file to optimize the design. Make targeted, incremental changes rather than rewriting files from scratch — small edits are less likely to introduce bugs.
- Write clean, synthesizable RTL code.
- You can pass `target_delay` (ps) to `run_evaluation` to try different synthesis timing constraints; experimenting with this may improve PPA results (lower target delay may reduce delay, higher target delay may reduce area/transistor count). target_delay must be > 0.
- Do not spend too many steps sweeping `target_delay` — basically if you want to minimize delay choose a low value,if you want to minimize area/transistor count choose a high value. That's it.A few targeted experiments are more productive than an exhaustive sweep.

## Delay Optimization Tips
- `target_delay` trades area against delay (a lower value pressures toward faster logic, a higher value lets Yosys re-map to smaller cells). Do not overly iterate on different target delays; often the RTL structure is the real lever, target_delay is only a minor secondary knob.
- After getting a correct design, try different accumulation orderings (sequential vs. tree) and compare results — Yosys synthesis can produce noticeably different delays (~10 ps) from structurally similar descriptions.
- For designs dominated by a multiply-accumulate chain, the critical path is the multiplier followed by the adder tree. Reducing the adder depth or splitting into parallel partial sums can help.

## Reference Implementations
The following are reference implementations for inspiration. Study them for patterns and techniques, but do not copy them directly — adapt ideas to your specific design.

### Parallel-prefix adder (ripple-carry / carry-lookahead) (Add.sv)
```systemverilog
// Copyright 2024 ETH Zurich and University of Bologna.
// Solderpad Hardware License, Version 0.51, see LICENSE for details.
// SPDX-License-Identifier: SHL-0.51
//
// Based on the work by Reto Zimmermann 1998 - ETH Zürich
// Originally written in VHDL, available under: 
// https://iis-people.ee.ethz.ch/~zimmi/arith_lib.html#library
//
// Authors:
// - Thomas Benz <tbenz@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
// - Philippe Sauter <phsauter@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
// - Paul Scheffler <paulsc@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
//
// Description :
// Binary adder using ripple-carry or parallel-prefix carry-lookahead logic.
// S = A+B

module Add #(
	parameter int              width = 8,             // word width
	parameter lau_pkg::speed_e speed = lau_pkg::FAST  // performance parameter
) (
	input  logic [width-1:0] A,  // operands
	input  logic [width-1:0] B,
	output logic [width-1:0] S   // sum
);

	// Function: Binary adder using parallel-prefix carry-lookahead logic.

	logic [width-1:0] GI, PI;  // prefix gen./prop. in
	logic [width-1:0] GO, PO;  // prefix gen./prop. out
	logic [width-1:0] PT;  // adder propagate temp

	// Internal signals for unsigned operands
	logic [width-1:0] Auns, Buns, Suns;

	// default ripple-carry adder as slow implementation
	if (speed == lau_pkg::SLOW) begin
		// type conversion: std_logic_vector -> unsigned
		assign Auns = A;
		assign Buns = B;

		// addition
		assign Suns = Auns + Buns;

		// type conversion: unsigned -> std_logic_vector
		assign S = Suns;
	end else begin
		// parallel-prefix adders as medium and fast implementations

		// calculate prefix input generate/propagate signals
		assign GI = A & B;
		assign PI = A | B;
		// calculate adder propagate signals (PT = A xor B)
		assign PT = ~GI & PI;

		// calculate prefix output generate/propagate signals
		PrefixAndOr #(
			.width(width),
			.speed(speed)
		) prefix (
			.GI(GI),
			.PI(PI),
			.GO(GO),
			.PO(PO)
		);

		// calculate sum bits
		assign S = PT ^ {GO[width-2:0], 1'b0};
	end
endmodule



module behavioural_Add #(
	parameter int              width = 8,             // word width
	parameter lau_pkg::speed_e speed = lau_pkg::FAST  // performance parameter
) (
	input  logic [width-1:0] A,  // operands
	input  logic [width-1:0] B,
	output logic [width-1:0] S   // sum
);
	assign S = A + B;
endmodule
```

### Prefix AND-OR structures (Sklansky, Brent-Kung, serial) (PrefixAndOr.sv)
```systemverilog
// Copyright 2024 ETH Zurich and University of Bologna.
// Solderpad Hardware License, Version 0.51, see LICENSE for details.
// SPDX-License-Identifier: SHL-0.51
//
// Based on the work by Reto Zimmermann 1998 - ETH Zürich
// Originally written in VHDL, available under: 
// https://iis-people.ee.ethz.ch/~zimmi/arith_lib.html#library
//
// Authors:
// - Thomas Benz <tbenz@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
// - Philippe Sauter <phsauter@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
// - Paul Scheffler <paulsc@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
//
// Description :
// Prefix structures of different depth (i.e. speed) for carry calculation
// in binary adders. Compute in m levels new generate/propagate signal pairs
// for always larger groups of bits. Generate signals of the last level correspond
// to carries in binary addition. Basic logic operations: AND-OR for generate
// signals, AND for propagate signals.

module PrefixAndOr #(
	parameter int              width = 8,             // word width
	parameter lau_pkg::speed_e speed = lau_pkg::FAST  // performance parameter
) (
	input  logic [width-1:0] GI,  // gen./prop. in
	input  logic [width-1:0] PI,  // gen./prop. in
	output logic [width-1:0] GO,  // gen./prop. out
	output logic [width-1:0] PO   // gen./prop. out
);

	// Constants
	localparam int n = width;  // prefix structure width
	localparam int m = $clog2(width);  // prefix structure depth

	// Sklansky parallel-prefix carry-lookahead structure
	if (speed == lau_pkg::FAST) begin : fastPrefix
		logic [(m+1)*n-1:0] GT, PT;  // gen./prop. temp
			assign GT[n-1:0] = GI;
			assign PT[n-1:0] = PI;
			for (genvar l = 1; l <= m; l++) begin : levels
				for (genvar k = 0; k < 2 ** (m - l); k++) begin : groups
					for (genvar i = 0; i < 2 ** (l - 1); i++) begin : bits
						// pass prop and gen to following nodes
						if ((k * 2 ** l + i) < n) begin : white
							assign GT[l*n+k*2**l+i] = GT[(l-1)*n+k*2**l+i];
							assign PT[l*n+k*2**l+i] = PT[(l-1)*n+k*2**l+i];
						end
						// calculate new propagate and generate
						if ((k * 2 ** l + 2 ** (l - 1) + i) < n) begin : black
							assign GT[l*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) + i] = 
												GT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) + i]
											  | (  PT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) + i]
												 & GT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) - 1] );
							assign PT[l*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) + i] = 
													PT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) + i]
												  & PT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) - 1];
						end
					end
				end
			end
			assign GO = GT[(m+1)*n-1 : m*n];
			assign PO = PT[(m+1)*n-1 : m*n];
	end

	// Brent-Kung parallel-prefix carry-lookahead structure
	if (speed == lau_pkg::MEDIUM) begin : mediumPrefix
		logic [(2*m)*n -1:0] GT, PT;  // gen./prop. temp
		assign GT[n-1:0] = GI;
		assign PT[n-1:0] = PI;

		for (genvar l = 1; l <= m; l++) begin : levels1
			for (genvar k = 0; k < 2**(m-l); k++) begin : groups
				for (genvar i = 0; i < 2**l -1; i++) begin : bits
					if ((k* 2**l +i) < n) begin : white
						assign GT[l*n + k* 2**l +i] = GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**l +i];
						assign PT[l*n + k* 2**l +i] = PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**l +i];
					end // white
				end // bits
				if ((k* 2**l + 2**l -1) < n) begin : black
					assign GT[l*n + k* 2**l + 2**l -1] =
								GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**l + 2**l - 1] |
							  | (  PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**l + 2**l     -1] 
							     & GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**l + 2**(l-1) -1]);
					assign PT[l*n + k* 2**l + 2**l -1] =
								PT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**l     -1] 
							  & PT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) -1];
				end // black
			end
		end // level1
		for (genvar l = m +1; l < 2*m; l++) begin : levels2
			for (genvar i = 0; i < 2**(2*m -l); i++) begin : bits
				if (i < n) begin : white
					assign GT[l*n +i] = GT[(l-1)*n +i];
					assign PT[l*n +i] = PT[(l-1)*n +i];
				end // white
			end // bits
			for (genvar k = 1; k < 2**(l-m); k++) begin : groups
				if (l < 2*m -1) begin : empty
					for (genvar i = 0; i < 2**(2*m -l -1) -1; i++) begin : bits
						if ((k* 2**(2*m -l) +i) < n) begin : white
							assign GT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i] = GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i];
							assign PT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i] = PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i];
						end // white
					end
				end // empty
				if ((k* 2**(2*m -l) + 2**(2*m -l -1) -1) < n) begin : black
					assign GT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) + 2**(2*m -l-1) -1] = 
								GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m-l) + 2**(2*m -l-1) -1]
							  | (  PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m-l) + 2**(2*m-l-1) -1] 
								 & GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m-l) -1] );
					assign PT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) + 2**(2*m -l-1) -1] = 
								PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) + 2**(2*m -l-1) -1]
							  & PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) -1];
				end // black
				for (genvar i = 2**(2*m -l-1); i < 2**(2*m -l); i++) begin : bits
					if ((k* 2**(2*m -l) +i) < n) begin : white
						assign GT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i] = GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i];
						assign PT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i] = PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i];
					end // white
				end
			end
		end // level2
		assign GO = GT[2*m*n -1 : (2*m -1) * n];
		assign PO = PT[2*m*n -1 : (2*m -1) * n];
	end  // Serial-prefix carry-lookahead structure
	else if (speed == lau_pkg::SLOW) begin : slowPrefix
		logic [n-1:0] GT, PT;  // gen./prop. temp
		assign GT[0] = GI[0];
		assign PT[0] = PI[0];
		
		for (genvar i = 1; i < n; i++) begin : bits
			assign GT[i] = GI[i] | (PI[i] & GT[i-1]);
			assign PT[i] = PI[i] & PT[i-1];
		end
		assign GO = GT;
		assign PO = PT;
	end

endmodule

```

[Step 1/60]
// fp_mul_e5f10 -- IEEE binary16 multiplier (1 sign, 5 exponent, 10 fraction bits) with subnormal support.

module fp_mul_e5f10 (a, b, y);
  input  [15:0] a;
  input  [15:0] b;
  output [15:0] y;

  localparam BIAS         = 15;
  localparam MAX_E        = 31;   // inf / NaN
  localparam MAX_FINITE_E = 30;
  localparam PROD_W       = 22;   // 11 x 11 bit significand product

  // 1. unpack
  wire       s_a = a[15];
  wire       s_b = b[15];
  wire [4:0] e_a = a[14:10];
  wire [4:0] e_b = b[14:10];
  wire [9:0] f_a = a[9:0];
  wire [9:0] f_b = b[9:0];

  // 2. classify
  wire e_a_zero = (e_a == 5'd0);
  wire e_b_zero = (e_b == 5'd0);
  wire e_a_ones = (e_a == MAX_E);
  wire e_b_ones = (e_b == MAX_E);
  wire zero_a   = e_a_zero & (f_a == 10'd0);
  wire zero_b   = e_b_zero & (f_b == 10'd0);
  wire nan_a    = e_a_ones & (f_a != 10'd0);
  wire nan_b    = e_b_ones & (f_b != 10'd0);
  wire inf_a    = e_a_ones & (f_a == 10'd0);
  wire inf_b    = e_b_ones & (f_b == 10'd0);

  wire nan_in  = nan_a | nan_b | (inf_a & zero_b) | (zero_a & inf_b);
  wire inf_in  = inf_a | inf_b;
  wire zero_in = zero_a | zero_b;
  wire s_y     = s_a ^ s_b;

  // 3. effective significands and exponents (subnormal: no hidden one, exponent 1)
  wire [10:0] m_a    = {~e_a_zero, f_a};
  wire [10:0] m_b    = {~e_b_zero, f_b};
  wire [4:0]  ea_eff = e_a_zero ? 5'd1 : e_a;
  wire [4:0]  eb_eff = e_b_zero ? 5'd1 : e_b;

  // 4. significand product, its leading-zero count (0 for a zero product), below[k] = |prod[k:0]
  wire [PROD_W-1:0] prod = m_a * m_b;

  reg [4:0] lz;
  integer   i;
  always @* begin
    lz = 5'd0;
    for (i = 0; i < PROD_W; i = i + 1)
      if (prod[i]) lz = 5'd21 - i[4:0];
  end

  wire [PROD_W-1:0] below;
  genvar k;
  generate
    for (k = 0; k < PROD_W; k = k + 1) begin : g_below
      assign below[k] = |prod[k:0];
    end
  endgenerate

  function bit_at;   // v[idx], 0 outside the vector
    input [PROD_W-1:0] v;
    input integer      idx;
    begin
      bit_at = (idx >= 0 && idx < PROD_W) ? v[idx] : 1'b0;
    end
  endfunction

  // 5. normalise and round a normal result: leading one onto bit 10
  wire              need_right = (lz <= 5'd11);
  wire [4:0]        sr         = 5'd11 - lz;   // right shift (wide product)
  wire [4:0]        sl         = lz - 5'd11;   // left shift (narrow product)
  wire [PROD_W-1:0] shifted    = need_right ? (prod >> sr) : (prod << sl);
  wire [10:0]       mant_pre   = shifted[10:0];

  wire guard    = need_right ? bit_at(prod,  sr - 1) : 1'b0;   // first bit shifted out
  wire sticky   = need_right ? bit_at(below, sr - 2) : 1'b0;   // any bit below the guard
  wire round_up = guard & (sticky | mant_pre[0]);              // round to nearest, ties to even

  wire [11:0] mant_round = {1'b0, mant_pre} + {11'd0, round_up};
  wire        carry      = mant_round[11];   // rounded up to a new leading one
  wire [10:0] mant_post  = carry ? mant_round[11:1] : mant_round[10:0];
  wire [9:0]  frac_norm  = mant_post[9:0];

  // 6. exponent of a normal result
  wire [7:0] exp_sum     = {3'd0, ea_eff} + {3'd0, eb_eff};
  wire [7:0] lhs         = exp_sum + 8'd1 + {7'd0, carry};
  wire [7:0] limit_under = BIAS + {3'd0, lz};
  wire [7:0] limit_over  = BIAS + MAX_FINITE_E + {3'd0, lz};
  wire       underflow   = (lhs <= limit_under);   // subnormal (or zero) result
  wire       overflow    = (lhs >  limit_over);    // past the largest finite number
  wire [7:0] e_norm      = lhs - limit_under;
  wire [4:0] exp_norm    = e_norm[4:0];

  // 7. subnormal result: shift the raw product onto the subnormal grid, round once
  integer           total_shift;
  reg [PROD_W-1:0]  sub_shifted;
  always @* begin
    total_shift = (BIAS + 10 + 1) - $signed({1'b0, exp_sum});
    sub_shifted = (total_shift >= 0 && total_shift < PROD_W) ? (prod >> total_shift) : {PROD_W{1'b0}};
  end
  wire [9:0]  frac_trunc = sub_shifted[9:0];
  wire        guard_s    = bit_at(prod,  total_shift - 1);
  wire        sticky_s   = bit_at(below, total_shift - 2);
  wire        round_s    = guard_s & (sticky_s | frac_trunc[0]);
  wire [10:0] frac_sum   = {1'b0, frac_trunc} + {10'd0, round_s};
  wire        carry_s    = frac_sum[10];   // rounded up to the smallest normal
  wire [9:0]  frac_sub   = frac_sum[9:0];
  wire [4:0]  exp_sub    = {4'd0, carry_s};

  // 8. pack
  wire is_nan      = nan_in;
  wire is_inf      = ~is_nan & (inf_in | (overflow & ~zero_in));
  wire is_sub      = ~is_nan & ~is_inf & underflow;
  wire sub_is_zero = is_sub & (exp_sub == 5'd0) & (frac_sub == 10'd0);
  wire is_zero     = ~is_nan & ~is_inf & (zero_in | sub_is_zero);

  wire [4:0] exp_y  = (is_nan | is_inf) ? 5'd31 : is_zero ? 5'd0 : is_sub ? exp_sub : exp_norm;
  wire [9:0] frac_y = is_nan ? 10'h200 : (is_inf | is_zero) ? 10'd0 : is_sub ? frac_sub : frac_norm;
  wire       sign_y = is_nan ? 1'b0 : s_y;

  assign y = {sign_y, exp_y, frac_y};
endmodule

`timescale 1ns/10ps
// exhaustive-directed tb (fpmul_run)
// Golden-referenced directed testbench: compares the DUT against the embedded
// golden reference (fp_mul_e5f10_ref, generated from context/starting_point.py)
// over ~18.9M enumerated vectors — no stimuli file. Structure:
//   1. the 3 known subnormal-boundary regression pairs (+ golden self-checks)
//   2. every subnormal/zero a (2^11 sign+mantissa patterns) x 4096 structured b
//   3. 4096 structured a x every subnormal/zero b
//   4. 2^21 LFSR-driven broad pairs
// Structured operands: both signs x all 32 exponents x 64 mantissas
// (8 corners + 56 LFSR-derived). The retired file-driven testbench is kept at
// tb_vectors.sv (+ vectors.dat) for cross-checking; it is not compiled.
module tb;
  logic [15:0] a, b, y, y_ref;
  int errors;
  longint total;

  fp_mul_e5f10 dut (.a(a), .b(b), .y(y));
  fp_mul_e5f10_ref refm (.a(a), .b(b), .y(y_ref));

  function automatic [15:0] lfsr_next(input [15:0] s);
    lfsr_next = (s >> 1) ^ (s[0] ? 16'hB400 : 16'h0000);
  endfunction

  logic [15:0] structured [0:4095];
  localparam logic [9:0] MANT_CORNERS [0:7] =
      '{10'h000, 10'h001, 10'h002, 10'h003, 10'h3FF, 10'h3FE, 10'h2AA, 10'h155};

  task automatic check();
    #1;
    total = total + 1;
    if (y !== y_ref) begin
      if (errors < 10)
        $display("TB_ERROR a=%0d b=%0d expected_y=%0d actual_y=%0d", a, b, y_ref, y);
      errors = errors + 1;
    end
  endtask

  logic [15:0] lfsr, ra, rb;
  logic [9:0] mant;
  int idx;

  initial begin
    errors = 0; total = 0;

    lfsr = 16'hACE1;
    idx = 0;
    for (int s = 0; s < 2; s++)
      for (int e = 0; e < 32; e++)
        for (int m = 0; m < 64; m++) begin
          if (m < 8) mant = MANT_CORNERS[m];
          else begin lfsr = lfsr_next(lfsr); mant = lfsr[9:0]; end
          structured[idx] = {s[0], e[4:0], mant};
          idx = idx + 1;
        end

    a = 16'h0003; b = 16'h6801; check();
    if (y_ref !== 16'h0E02) begin errors++; $display("TB_ERROR golden self-check 1"); end
    a = 16'h8003; b = 16'h6479; check();
    if (y_ref !== 16'h8AB6) begin errors++; $display("TB_ERROR golden self-check 2"); end
    a = 16'h7521; b = 16'h0003; check();
    if (y_ref !== 16'h1BB2) begin errors++; $display("TB_ERROR golden self-check 3"); end

    for (int sa = 0; sa < 2048; sa++) begin
      a = {sa[10], 5'b00000, sa[9:0]};
      for (int i = 0; i < 4096; i++) begin b = structured[i]; check(); end
    end
    for (int i = 0; i < 4096; i++) begin
      a = structured[i];
      for (int sb = 0; sb < 2048; sb++) begin b = {sb[10], 5'b00000, sb[9:0]}; check(); end
    end
    ra = 16'h0001; rb = 16'hBEEF;
    for (int k = 0; k < (1 << 21); k++) begin
      ra = lfsr_next(ra); rb = lfsr_next(lfsr_next(rb));
      a = ra; b = rb; check();
    end

    $display("TB_SUMMARY total=%0d errors=%0d", total, errors);
    if (errors != 0) $fatal(1, "FAIL");
    $display("PASS");

  -> create_file(filename='design.sv', content="// fp_mul_e5f10 -- IEEE binary16 multiplier (1 sign, 5 exponent, 10 fraction bits) with subnormal support.\n\nmodule fp_mul_e5f10 (a, b, y);\n  input  [15:0] a;\n  input  [15:0] b;\n  output [15:0] y;\n\n  localparam BIAS         = 15;\n  localparam MAX_E        = 31;   // inf / NaN\n  localparam MAX_FINITE_E = 30;\n  localparam PROD_W       = 22;   // 11 x 11 bit significand product\n\n  // 1. unpack\n  wire       s_a = a[15];\n  wire       s_b = b[15];\n  wire [4:0] e_a = a[14:10];\n  wire [4:0] e_b = b[14:10];\n  wire [9:0] f_a = a[9:0];\n  wire [9:0] f_b = b[9:0];\n\n  // 2. classify\n  wire e_a_zero = (e_a == 5'd0);\n  wire e_b_zero = (e_b == 5'd0);\n  wire e_a_ones = (e_a == MAX_E);\n  wire e_b_ones = (e_b == MAX_E);\n  wire zero_a   = e_a_zero & (f_a == 10'd0);\n  wire zero_b   = e_b_zero & (f_b == 10'd0);\n  wire nan_a    = e_a_ones & (f_a != 10'd0);\n  wire nan_b    = e_b_ones & (f_b != 10'd0);\n  wire inf_a    = e_a_ones & (f_a == 10'd0);\n  wire inf_b    = e_b_ones & (f_b == 10'd0);\n\n  wire nan_in  = nan_a | nan_b | (inf_a & zero_b) | (zero_a & inf_b);\n  wire inf_in  = inf_a | inf_b;\n  wire zero_in = zero_a | zero_b;\n  wire s_y     = s_a ^ s_b;\n\n  // 3. effective significands and exponents (subnormal: no hidden one, exponent 1)\n  wire [10:0] m_a    = {~e_a_zero, f_a};\n  wire [10:0] m_b    = {~e_b_zero, f_b};\n  wire [4:0]  ea_eff = e_a_zero ? 5'd1 : e_a;\n  wire [4:0]  eb_eff = e_b_zero ? 5'd1 : e_b;\n\n  // 4. significand product, its leading-zero count (0 for a zero product), below[k] = |prod[k:0]\n  wire [PROD_W-1:0] prod = m_a * m_b;\n\n  reg [4:0] lz;\n  integer   i;\n  always @* begin\n    lz = 5'd0;\n    for (i = 0; i < PROD_W; i = i + 1)\n      if (prod[i]) lz = 5'd21 - i[4:0];\n  end\n\n  wire [PROD_W-1:0] below;\n  genvar k;\n  generate\n    for (k = 0; k < PROD_W; k = k + 1) begin : g_below\n      assign below[k] = |prod[k:0];\n    end\n  endgenerate\n\n  function bit_at;   // v[idx], 0 outside the vector\n    input [PROD_W-1:0] v;\n    input integer      idx;\n    begin\n      bit_at = (idx >= 0 && idx < PROD_W) ? v[idx] : 1'b0;\n    end\n  endfunction\n\n  // 5. normalise and round a normal result: leading one onto bit 10\n  wire              need_right = (lz <= 5'd11);\n  wire [4:0]        sr         = 5'd11 - lz;   // right shift (wide product)\n  wire [4:0]        sl         = lz - 5'd11;   // left shift (narrow product)\n  wire [PROD_W-1:0] shifted    = need_right ? (prod >> sr) : (prod << sl);\n  wire [10:0]       mant_pre   = shifted[10:0];\n\n  wire guard    = need_right ? bit_at(prod,  sr - 1) : 1'b0;   // first bit shifted out\n  wire sticky   = need_right ? bit_at(below, sr - 2) : 1'b0;   // any bit below the guard\n  wire round_up = guard & (sticky | mant_pre[0]);              // round to nearest, ties to even\n\n  wire [11:0] mant_round = {1'b0, mant_pre} + {11'd0, round_up};\n  wire        carry      = mant_round[11];   // rounded up to a new leading one\n  wire [10:0] mant_post  = carry ? mant_round[11:1] : mant_round[10:0];\n  wire [9:0]  frac_norm  = mant_post[9:0];\n\n  // 6. exponent of a normal result\n  wire [7:0] exp_sum     = {3'd0, ea_eff} + {3'd0, eb_eff};\n  wire [7:0] lhs         = exp_sum + 8'd1 + {7'd0, carry};\n  = exp_sum + 8'd1 + {7'd0, carry};\n  wire [7:0] limit_under = BIAS + {3'd0, lz};\n  wire [7:0] limit_over  = BIAS + MAX_FINITE_E + {3'd0, lz};\n  wire       underflow   = (lhs <= limit_under);   // subnormal (or zero) result\n  wire       overflow    = (lhs >  limit_over);    // past the largest finite number\n  wire [7:0] e_norm      = lhs - limit_under;\n  wire [4:0] exp_norm    = e_norm[4:0];\n\n  // 7. subnormal result: shift the raw product onto the subnormal grid, round once\n  integer           total_shift;\n  reg [PROD_W-1:0]  sub_shifted;\n  always @* begin\n    total_shift = (BIAS + 10 + 1) - $signed({1'b0, exp_sum});\n    sub_shifted = (total_shift >= 0 && total_shift < PROD_W) ? (prod >> total_shift) : {PROD_W{1'b0}};\n  end\n  wire [9:0]  frac_trunc = sub_shifted[9:0];\n  wire        guard_s    = bit_at(prod,  total_shift - 1);\n  wire        sticky_s   = bit_at(below, total_shift - 2);\n  wire        round_s    = guard_s & (sticky_s | frac_trunc[0]);\n  wire [10:0] frac_sum   = {1'b0, frac_trunc} + {10'd0, round_s};\n  wire        carry_s    = frac_sum[10];   // rounded up to the smallest normal\n  wire [9:0]  frac_sub   = frac_sum[9:0];\n  wire [4:0]  exp_sub    = {4'd0, carry_s};\n\n  // 8. pack\n  wire is_nan      = nan_in;\n  wire is_inf      = ~is_nan & (inf_in | (overflow & ~zero_in));\n  wire is_sub      = ~is_nan & ~is_inf & underflow;\n  wire sub_is_zero = is_sub & (exp_sub == 5'd0) & (frac_sub == 10'd0);\n  wire is_zero     = ~is_nan & ~is_inf & (zero_in | sub_is_zero);\n\n  wire [4:0] exp_y  = (is_nan | is_inf) ? 5'd31 : is_zero ? 5'd0 : is_sub ? exp_sub : exp_norm;\n  wire [9:0] frac_y = is_nan ? 10'h200 : (is_inf | is_zero) ? 10'd0 : is_sub ? frac_sub : frac_norm;\n  wire       sign_y = is_nan ? 1'b0 : s_y;\n\n  assign y = {sign_y, exp_y, frac_y};\nendmodule\n")

[ASSISTANT]
The baseline passes with ADP = 145429 (area 108.7, delay 1338 ps). The critical path runs through the exponent adder → barrel shifters → rounding/packing. The design has three wide barrel shifters and a 22-bit priority LZC — big area/delay contributors.

My optimization plan:
1. **Pre-normalize subnormal inputs** (small LZC + 11-bit shift on inputs) so the product is always in [2²⁰, 2²²) — eliminates the product-side LZC and the left-shifter entirely; normalization becomes a single 2:1 mux.
2. The subnormal-result path uses a single small right shift (fixed wiring + 4-bit-select shift), with G/S tapped from prefix-OR.
3. Elegant packing: rounded subnormal fraction's carry bit becomes the exponent LSB directly.

Let me write this variant:
  -> create_file(filename='design_v2.sv', content="// fp_mul_e5f10 -- IEEE binary16 (1+5+10) multiplier, subnormals supported, RNE.\n// Strategy: pre-normalize subnormal operands (LZC + 11-bit left shift),\n// multiply normalized 11-bit significands (product in [2^20, 2^22)),\n// single-bit normalisation, one rounding, compact subnormal right shift.\n\nmodule lzc10 (input [9:0] f, output [3:0] c);\n  wire [15:0] F  = {f, 6'b0};\n  wire        c3 = ~|F[15:8];\n  wire [7:0]  F2 = c3 ? F[7:0] : F[15:8];\n  wire        c2 = ~|F2[7:4];\n  wire [3:0]  F3 = c2 ? F2[3:0] : F2[7:4];\n  wire        c1 = ~|F3[3:2];\n  wire [1:0]  F4 = c1 ? F3[1:0] : F3[3:2];\n  wire        c0 = ~F4[1];\n  assign c = {c3, c2, c1, c0};\nendmodule\n\nmodule fp_mul_e5f10 (a, b, y);\n  input  [15:0] a;\n  input  [15:0] b;\n  output [15:0] y;\n\n  wire        s_a = a[15];\n  wire        s_b = b[15];\n  wire [4:0]  e_a = a[14:10];\n  wire [4:0]  e_b = b[14:10];\n  wire [9:0]  f_a = a[9:0];\n  wire [9:0]  f_b = b[9:0];\n\n  wire e_a_zero = (e_a == 5'd0);\n  wire e_b_zero = (e_b == 5'd0);\n  wire e_a_ones = (e_a == 5'd31);\n  wire e_b_ones = (e_b == 5'd31);\n  wire zero_a = e_a_zero & (f_a == 10'd0);\n  wire zero_b = e_b_zero & (f_b == 10'd0);\n  wire nan_a  = e_a_ones & (f_a != 10'd0);\n  wire nan_b  = e_b_ones & (f_b != 10'd0);\n  wire inf_a  = e_a_ones & (f_a == 10'd0);\n  wire inf_b  = e_b_ones & (f_b == 10'd0);\n\n  wire nan_in  = nan_a | nan_b | (inf_a & zero_b) | (zero_a & inf_b);\n  wire inf_in  = inf_a | inf_b;\n  wire zero_in = zero_a | zero_b;\n  wire s_y     = s_a ^ s_b;\n\n  wire [3:0] lzc_a, lzc_b;\n  lzc10 u_lzc_a (.f(f_a), .c(lzc_a));\n  lzc10 u_lzc_b (.f(f_b), .c(lzc_b));\n\n  // normalized significands (hidden one at bit 10) and signed exponents\n  wire [10:0] m_a = e_a_zero ? ({f_a, 1'b0} << lzc_a) : {1'b1, f_a};\n  wire [10:0] m_b = e_b_zero ? ({f_b, 1'b0} << lzc_b) : {1'b1, f_b};\n  wire signed [7:0] E_a   = e_a_zero ? -$signed({4'd0, lzc_a}) : $signed({3'd0, e_a});\n  wire signed [7:0] E_b   = e_b_zero ? -$signed({4'd0, lzc_b}) : $signed({3'd0, e_b});\n  wire signed [7:0] E_sum = E_a + E_b;\n\n  wire [21:0] prod = m_a * m_b;\n  wire norm = prod[21];\n\n  // below[j] = |prod[j:0], saturated above bit 21\n  wire [24:0] below;\n  genvar k;\n  generate\n    for (k = 0; k < 25; k = k + 1) begin : g_below\n      assign below[k] = (k < 22) ? |prod[k:0] : |prod[21:0];\n    end\n  endgenerate\n\n  // --- normal result path -------------------------------------------------\n  wire [10:0] mant_pre  = norm ? prod[21:11] : prod[20:10];\n  wire        G_n       = norm ? prod[10] : prod[9];\n  wire        S_n       = norm ? below[9] : below[8];\n  wire        round_n   = G_n & (S_n | mant_pre[0]);\n  wire [11:0] mant_sum  = {1'b0, mant_pre} + {11'd0, round_n};\n  wire        cy        = mant_sum[11];\n  wire [9:0]  frac_n    = cy ? 4'd15 : (4'd15 - E_sum[3:0]);\n  wire [15:0] w_ext  = {4'd0, prod[21:10]};        // prod >> 10\n  wire [15:0] w_sh   = w_ext >> ({1'b0, x} + 5'd1);\n  wire [9:0]  frac_t = w_sh[9:0];\n  wire        G_s    = w_ext[x];\n  wire        S_s    = below[{1'b0, x} + 5'd9];\n  wire        round_s  = G_s & (S_s | frac_t[0]);\n  wire [10:0] frac_sum = {1'b0, frac_t} + {10'd0, round_s};\n\n  // --- pack ---------------------------------------------------------------\n  wire [15:0] y_norm = {s_y, E_y[4:0], frac_n};\n  wire [15:0] y_sub  = {s_y, 4'd0, frac_sum};\n\n  assign y = nan_in    ? 16'h7E00 :\n             inf_in    ? {s_y, 5'd31, 10'd0} :\n             zero_in   ? {s_y, 15'd0} :\n             overflow  ? {s_y, 5'd31, 10'd0} :\n             sub_res   ? y_sub :\n                         y_norm;\nendmodule\n")

For v3 I'll:

({f_a, 1'b0} << lzc_a) : {1'b1, f_a};\n  wire [10:0] m_b = e_b_zero ? ({f_b, 1'b0} << lzc_b) : {1'b1, f_b};\n  wire signed [7:0] E_a = e_a_zero ? |prod[k:0] : |prod[21:0];\n    end\n  endgenerate\n\n  // --- normal result path -------------------------------------------------\n  wire [10:0] mant_pre  = norm ? prod[21:11] : prod[20:10];\n  wire        G_n       = norm ? prod[10] : prod[9];\n  wire        S_n       = norm ? below[9] : below[8];\n  wire        round_n   = G_n & (S_n | mant_pre[0]);\n  wire [11:0] mant_sum  = {1'b0, mant_pre} + {11'd0, round_n};\n  wire        cy        = mant_sum[11];\n  wire [9:0]  frac_n    = cy ? 5'd31 :\n                      sub_res ? {4'd0, frac_sum[10]} : ey5;\n  wire [9:0] frac_y = nan_in  ? 10'h200 :\n                      spec_f  ? 10'd0 :\n                      sub_res ? frac_sum[9:0] : frac_n;\n  wire sign_y = nan_in ? 1'b0 : s_y;\n\n  assign y = {sign_y, exp_y, frac_y};\nendmodule\n", filename='design_v3.sv')

[ASSISTANT]
v3 improves to ADP 123349 (delay 1319). The critical path still contains a long ripple-carry exponent adder (XOR/XNOR + MAJI chain) and comparator logic in the pack tail.

For v4, I'll restructure the exponent arithmetic:
- Explicit **parallel-prefix (Kogge-Stone) adders** instead of relying on `+` (which ABC maps as ripple).
- Compute `sub_res` directly from `W0 = E_a + E_b + norm` bit formulas (2 gate levels), removing a chained adder from the select path.
- Express `ovf`/`ey5` from `W0` with constant folds.

[ASSISTANT]
v4 is a wash vs v3 (delay −45ps but area +4%). Best is still v3. 

Now a structural insight: the full exponent adder feeds the shifter **select** (`x`), serializing adder→shifter. But `x = ~E_sum[3:0]` only needs the low 4 sum bits (fast partial add); the slow sign bit (`E_sum ≤ 0`) is only needed to force the result to zero — that can be a data mux **in parallel** with the shifter stages. Let me build v5 (based on v4) with this de-serialization:
  -> create_file(content="// fp_mul_e5f10 -- IEEE binary16 (1+5+10) multiplier, subnormals supported, RNE.\n// v5: pre-normalized operands, parallel-prefix adders, de-serialized subnormal\n// shifter: select uses only low sum bits; sign bit forces zero in parallel.\n\n// parallel-prefix (Kogge-Stone style) adder\nmodule padd #(parameter W = 8) (\n  input  [W-1:0] A,\n  input  [W-1:0] B,\n  input          Cin,\n  output [W-1:0] S,\n  output         Cout\n);\n  wire [W-1:0] g = A & B;\n  wire [W-1:0] p = A ^ B;\n  localparam L = (W <= 2) ? 1 : $clog2(W);\n  wire [W-1:0] G [0:L];\n  wire [W-1:0] P [0:L];\n  assign G[0] = g;\n  assign P[0] = p;\n  genvar i, l;\n  generate\n    for (l = 1; l <= L; l = l + 1) begin : lvl\n      for (i = 0; i < W; i = i + 1) begin : b\n        if (i >= (1 << (l-1))) begin : hi\n          assign G[l][i] = G[l-1][i] | (P[l-1][i] & G[l-1][i - (1 << (l-1))]);\n          assign P[l][i] = P[l-1][i] & P[l-1][i - (1 << (l-1))];\n        end else begin : lo\n          assign G[l][i] = G[l-1][i];\n          assign P[l][i] = P[l-1][i];\n        end\n      end\n    end\n  endgenerate\n  wire [W-1:0] carry;\n  assign carry[0] = Cin;\n  generate\n    for (i = 1; i < W; i = i + 1) begin : c\n      assign carry[i] = G[L][i-1] | (P[L][i-1] & Cin);\n    end\n  endgenerate\n  assign S = p ^ carry;\n  assign Cout = G[L][W-1] | (P[L][W-1] & Cin);\nendmodule\n\nmodule lzc10 (input [9:0] f, output [3:0] c);\n  wire [15:0] F  = {f, 6'b0};\n  wire        c3 = ~|F[15:8];\n  wire [7:0]  F2 = c3 ? F[7:0] : F[15:8];\n  wire        c2 = ~|F2[7:4];\n  wire [3:0]  F3 = c2 ? F2[3:0] : F2[7:4];\n  wire        c1 = ~|F3[3:2];\n  wire [1:0]  F4 = c1 ? F3[1:0] : F3[3:2];\n  wire        c0 = ~F4[1];\n  assign c = {c3, c2, c1, c0};\nendmodule\n\nmodule fp_mul_e5f10 (a, b, y);\n  input  [15:0] a;\n  input  [15:0] b;\n  output [15:0] y;\n\n  wire        s_a = a[15];\n  wire        s_b = b[15];\n  wire [4:0]  e_a = a[14:10];\n  wire [4:0]  e_b = b[14:10];\n  wire [9:0]  f_a = a[9:0];\n  wire [9:0]  f_b = b[9:0];\n\n  wire e_a_zero = (e_a == 5'd0);\n  wire e_b_zero = (e_b == 5'd0);\n  wire e_a_ones = (e_a == 5'd31);\n  wire e_b_ones = (e_b == 5'd31);\n  wire zero_a = e_a_zero & (f_a == 10'd0);\n  wire zero_b = e_b_zero & (f_b == 10'd0);\n  wire nan_a  = e_a_ones & (f_a != 10'd0);\n  wire nan_b  = e_b_ones & (f_b != 10'd0);\n  wire inf_a  = e_a_ones & (f_a == 10'd0);\n  wire inf_b  = e_b_ones & (f_b == 10'd0);\n\n  wire nan_in  = nan_a | nan_b | (inf_a & zero_b) | (zero_a & inf_b);\n  wire inf_in  = inf_a | inf_b;\n  wire s_y     = s_a ^ s_b;\n\n  wire [3:0] lzc_a, lzc_b;\n  lzc10 u_lzc_a (.f(f_a), .c(lzc_a));\n  lzc10 u_lzc_b (.f(f_b), .c(lzc_b));\n\n  // normalized significands (hidden one at bit 10) and signed exponents.\n  // Zero operands get a very negative exponent so the arithmetic path\n  // naturally produces a signed zero (product is 0 anyway).\n  wire [10:0] m_a = e_a_zero ? ({f_a, 1'b0} << lzc_a) : {1'b1, f_a};\n  wire [10:0] m_b = e_b_zero ? ({f_b, 1'b0} << lzc_b) : {1'b1, f_b};\n  wire signed [6:0] E_a = e_a_zero ? (zero_a ? -7'sd20 : -$signed({3'd0, lzc_a}))\n                                   : $signed({2'd0, e_a});\n  wire signed [6:0] E_b = e_b_zero ? (zero_b ? -7'sd20 : -$signed({3'd0, lzc_b}))\n                                   : $signed({2'd0, e_b});\n\n  wire [21:0] prod = m_a * m_b;\n  wire norm = prod[21];\n\n  // E_sum = E_a + E_b ; W0 = E_a + E_b + norm\n  wire signed [6:0] E_sum, W0;\n  wire       es_co, w0_co;\n  padd #(.W(7)) u_es (.A(E_a), .B(E_b), .Cin(1'b0), .S(E_sum), .Cout(es_co));\n  padd #(.W(7)) u_w0 (.A(E_a), .B(E_b), .Cin(norm), .S(W0), .Cout(w0_co));\n\n  // below[j] = |prod[j:0], saturated above bit 21\n  wire [24:0] below;\n  genvar k;\n  generate\n    for (k = 0; k < 25; k = k + 1) begin : g_below\n      assign below[k] = (k < 22) ? |prod[k:0] : |prod[21:0];\n    end\n  endgenerate\n\n  // --- normal result path -------------------------------------------------\n  wire [10:0] mant_pre = norm ? prod[21:11] : prod[20:10];\n  wire        G_n      = norm ? prod[10] : prod[9];\n  wire        S_n      = norm ? below[9] : below[8];\n  wire        round_n  = G_n & (S_n | mant_pre[0]);\n  wire [10:0] mant_post;\n  wire        cy;\n  padd #(.W(11)) u_mant (.A(mant_pre), .B(11'd0), .Cin(round_n), .S(mant_post), .Cout(cy));\n  wire [9:0] frac_n = cy ? mant_post[10:1] : mant_post[9:0];\n\n  // predicates from W0 = E_a + E_b + norm (pre-rounding biased exponent + 15)\n  wire sub_res = W0[6] | ~|W0[6:4];                    // W0 <= 15\n  wire ovf     = (W0 > 7'sd45) | ((W0 == 7'sd45) & cy);\n  wire [4:0] ey5 = W0[4:0] + (cy ? 5'd18 : 5'd17);     // W0 + cy - 15\n\n  // --- subnormal result path ---------------------------------------------\n  // value = prod * 2^(E_sum-50); subnormal frac = prod >> (26 - E_sum)\n  // 26 - E_sum = 11 + x  with  x = 15 - E_sum  (for E_sum in [1,15]).\n  // The shifter select uses only the low sum bits; the sign of E_sum forces\n  // a zero result in parallel with the shift (E_sum <= 0 => value tiny).\n  wire        zforce  = E_sum[6] | ~|E_sum[5:0];       // E_sum <= 0\n  wire [3:0]  x       = ~E_sum[3:0];\n  wire [15:0] w_ext   = {4'd0, prod[21:10]};           // prod >> 10\n  wire [15:0] w_sh    = w_ext >> ({1'b0, x} + 5'd1);\n  wire [9:0]  frac_t  = zforce ? 10'd0 : w_sh[9:0];\n  wire        G_s     = zforce ? 1'b0 : w_ext[x];\n  wire        S_s     = below[{1'b0, x} + 5'd9];\n  wire        round_s = G_s & (S_s | frac_t[0]);\n  wire [9:0]  frac_sum;\n  wire        fs_co;\n  padd #(.W(10)) u_fs (.A(frac_t), .B(10'd0), .Cin(round_s), .S(frac_sum), .Cout(fs_co));\n\n  // --- pack ---------------------------------------------------------------\n  wire spec_e = nan_in | inf_in | ovf;    // exponent field 31\n  wire spec_f = inf_in | ovf;             // fraction field 0 (non-NaN specials)\n\n  wire [4:0] exp_y  = spec_e ? 5'd31 :\n                      sub_res ? {4'd0, fs_co} : ey5;\n  wire [9:0] frac_y = nan_in  ? 10'h200 :\n                      spec_f  ? 10'd0 :\n                      sub_res ? frac_sum : frac_n;\n  wire sign_y = nan_in ? 1'b0 : s_y;\n\n  assign y = {sign_y, exp_y, frac_y};\nendmodule\n", filename='design_v5.sv')

[ASSISTANT]
v5 is again ≈ v3 (ADP 123881 vs 123349) — the prefix adders cost area while the multiplier became the critical path. Since in v5's structure the shifter select only needs low sum bits and the sign is parallel, the adder can be a cheap ripple (`+`) — ABC maps it small. Let me make v6 = v5 with plain `+` adders (keeping the de-serialized structure and flat predicates):
  -> create_file(content="// fp_mul_e5f10 -- IEEE binary16 (1+5+10) multiplier, subnormals supported, RNE.\n// v6: pre-normalized operands, de-serialized subnormal shifter (select uses\n// only low sum bits; sign bit forces zero in parallel), flat bit predicates.\n\nmodule lzc10 (input [9:0] f, output [3:0] c);\n  wire [15:0] F  = {f, 6'b0};\n  wire        c3 = ~|F[15:8];\n  wire [7:0]  F2 = c3 ? F[7:0] : F[15:8];\n  wire        c2 = ~|F2[7:4];\n  wire [3:0]  F3 = c2 ? F2[3:0] : F2[7:4];\n  wire        c1 = ~|F3[3:2];\n  wire [1:0]  F4 = c1 ? F3[1:0] : F3[3:2];\n  wire        c0 = ~F4[1];\n  assign c = {c3, c2, c1, c0};\nendmodule\n\nmodule fp_mul_e5f10 (a, b, y);\n  input  [15:0] a;\n  input  [15:0] b;\n  output [15:0] y;\n\n  wire        s_a = a[15];\n  wire        s_b = b[15];\n  wire [4:0]  e_a = a[14:10];\n  wire [4:0]  e_b = b[14:10];\n  wire [9:0]  f_a = a[9:0];\n  wire [9:0]  f_b = b[9:0];\n\n  wire e_a_zero = (e_a == 5'd0);\n  wire e_b_zero = (e_b == 5'd0);\n  wire e_a_ones = (e_a == 5'd31);\n  wire e_b_ones = (e_b == 5'd31);\n  wire zero_a = e_a_zero & (f_a == 10'd0);\n  wire zero_b = e_b_zero & (f_b == 10'd0);\n  wire nan_a  = e_a_ones & (f_a != 10'd0);\n  wire nan_b  = e_b_ones & (f_b != 10'd0);\n  wire inf_a  = e_a_ones & (f_a == 10'd0);\n  wire inf_b  = e_b_ones & (f_b == 10'd0);\n\n  wire nan_in  = nan_a | nan_b | (inf_a & zero_b) | (zero_a & inf_b);\n  wire inf_in  = inf_a | inf_b;\n  wire s_y     = s_a ^ s_b;\n\n  wire [3:0] lzc_a, lzc_b;\n  lzc10 u_lzc_a (.f(f_a), .c(lzc_a));\n  lzc10 u_lzc_b (.f(f_b), .c(lzc_b));\n\n  // normalized significands (hidden one at bit 10) and signed exponents.\n  // Zero operands get a very negative exponent so the arithmetic path\n  // naturally produces a signed zero (product is 0 anyway).\n  wire [10:0] m_a = e_a_zero ? ({f_a, 1'b0} << lzc_a) : {1'b1, f_a};\n  wire [10:0] m_b = e_b_zero ? ({f_b, 1'b0} << lzc_b) : {1'b1, f_b};\n  wire signed [6:0] E_a = e_a_zero ? (zero_a ? -7'sd20 : -$signed({3'd0, lzc_a}))\n                                   : $signed({2'd0, e_a});\n  wire signed [6:0] E_b = e_b_zero ? (zero_b ? -7'sd20 : -$signed({3'd0, lzc_b}))\n                                   : $signed({2'd0, e_b});\n\n  wire [21:0] prod = m_a * m_b;\n  wire norm = prod[21];\n\n  // E_sum = E_a + E_b ; W0 = E_a + E_b + norm\n  wire signed [6:0] E_sum = E_a + E_b;\n  wire signed [6:0] W0    = E_sum + {6'd0, norm};\n\n  // below[j] = |prod[j:0], saturated above bit 21\n  wire [24:0] below;\n  genvar k;\n  generate\n    for (k = 0; k < 25; k = k + 1) begin : g_below\n      assign below[k] = (k < 22) ? |prod[k:0] : |prod[21:0];\n    end\n  endgenerate\n\n  // --- normal result path -------------------------------------------------\n  wire [10:0] mant_pre = norm ? prod[21:11] : prod[20:10];\n  wire        G_n      = norm ? prod[10] : prod[9];\n  wire        S_n      = norm ? below[9] : below[8];\n  wire        round_n  = G_n & (S_n | mant_pre[0]);\n  wire [11:0] mant_sum = {1'b0, mant_pre} + {11'd0, round_n};\n  wire        cy       = mant_sum[11];\n  wire [9:0]  frac_n   = cy ? mant_sum[10:1] : mant_sum[9:0];\n\n  // predicates from W0 = E_a + E_b + norm (pre-rounding biased exponent + 15)\n  wire sub_res = W0[6] | ~|W0[6:4];                    // W0 <= 15\n  wire ovf     = (W0 > 7'sd45) | ((W0 == 7'sd45) & cy);\n  wire [4:0] ey5 = W0[4:0] + (cy ? 5'd18 : 5'd17);     // W0 + cy - 15\n\n  // --- subnormal result path ---------------------------------------------\n  // value = prod * 2^(E_sum-50); subnormal frac = prod >> (26 - E_sum)\n  // 26 - E_sum = 11 + x  with  x = 15 - E_sum  (for E_sum in [1,15]).\n  // The shifter select uses only the low sum bits; the sign of E_sum forces\n  // a zero result in parallel with the shift (E_sum <= 0 => value tiny).\n  wire        zforce  = E_sum[6] | ~|E_sum[5:0];       // E_sum <= 0\n  wire [3:0]  x       = ~E_sum[3:0];\n  wire [15:0] w_ext   = {4'd0, prod[21:10]};           // prod >> 10\n  wire [15:0] w_sh    = w_ext >> ({1'b0, x} + 5'd1);\n  wire [9:0]  frac_t  = zforce ? 10'd0 : w_sh[9:0];\n  wire        G_s     = zforce ? 1'b0 : w_ext[x];\n  wire        S_s     = below[{1'b0, x} + 5'd9];\n  wire        round_s = G_s & (S_s | frac_t[0]);\n  wire [10:0] frac_sum = {1'b0, frac_t} + {10'd0, round_s};\n  wire        fs_co    = frac_sum[10];\n\n  // --- pack ---------------------------------------------------------------\n  wire spec_e = nan_in | inf_in | ovf;    // exponent field 31\n  wire spec_f = inf_in | ovf;             // fraction field 0 (non-NaN specials)\n\n  wire [4:0] exp_y  = spec_e ? 5'd31 :\n                      sub_res ? {4'd0, fs_co} : ey5;\n  wire [9:0] frac_y = nan_in  ? 10'h200 :\n                      spec_f  ? 10'd0 :\n                      sub_res ? frac_sum[9:0] : frac_n;\n  wire sign_y = nan_in ? 1'b0 : s_y;\n\n  assign y = {sign_y, exp_y, frac_y};\nendmodule\n", filename='design_v6.sv')

[ASSISTANT]
The target_delay knob gives nothing here (same netlist at 500/1200; 1800 is worse). The real levers are structural. 

Now a significant restructure (v7): instead of pre-normalizing operands (barrel shifters on the multiplier inputs = 230ps on the critical path), multiply the **raw** significands and do ONE unified window extraction from the product:
- `R = r_a * r_b` (multiplier inputs are pure wiring — fastest possible mult path)
- Single extractor window at offset `u = sub_res ? (26-J) : (10-k+norm)` serving BOTH the normal mantissa and subnormal fraction, with G below the window and S from a prefix-OR.
- Unified rounder: since `window[10]=0` exactly in the subnormal case, one adder serves both paths (its bit-10 carry becomes the subnormal exponent LSB).
  -> create_file(content="// fp_mul_e5f10 -- IEEE binary16 (1+5+10) multiplier, subnormals supported, RNE.\n// v7: raw-significand multiply (multiplier inputs are wiring), one unified\n// window extractor serves both the normal and subnormal result paths.\n\nmodule lzc11 (input [10:0] r, output [3:0] c);\n  wire [15:0] F  = {r, 5'b0};\n  wire        c3 = ~|F[15:8];\n  wire [7:0]  F2 = c3 ? F[7:0] : F[15:8];\n  wire        c2 = ~|F2[7:4];\n  wire [3:0]  F3 = c2 ? F2[3:0] : F2[7:4];\n  wire        c1 = ~|F3[3:2];\n  wire [1:0]  F4 = c1 ? F3[1:0] : F3[3:2];\n  wire        c0 = ~F4[1];\n  assign c = {c3, c2, c1, c0};\nendmodule\n\nmodule fp_mul_e5f10 (a, b, y);\n  input  [15:0] a;\n  input  [15:0] b;\n  output [15:0] y;\n\n  wire        s_a = a[15];\n  wire        s_b = b[15];\n  wire [4:0]  e_a = a[14:10];\n  wire [4:0]  e_b = b[14:10];\n  wire [9:0]  f_a = a[9:0];\n  wire [9:0]  f_b = b[9:0];\n\n  wire e_a_zero = (e_a == 5'd0);\n  wire e_b_zero = (e_b == 5'd0);\n  wire e_a_ones = (e_a == 5'd31);\n  wire e_b_ones = (e_b == 5'd31);\n  wire zero_a = e_a_zero & (f_a == 10'd0);\n  wire zero_b = e_b_zero & (f_b == 10'd0);\n  wire nan_a  = e_a_ones & (f_a != 10'd0);\n  wire nan_b  = e_b_ones & (f_b != 10'd0);\n  wire inf_a  = e_a_ones & (f_a == 10'd0);\n  wire inf_b  = e_b_ones & (f_b == 10'd0);\n\n  wire nan_in  = nan_a | nan_b | (inf_a & zero_b) | (zero_a & inf_b);\n  wire inf_in  = inf_a | inf_b;\n  wire s_y     = s_a ^ s_b;\n\n  // raw significands and their leading-zero counts (= pre-normalization shifts)\n  wire [10:0] r_a = {~e_a_zero, f_a};\n  wire [10:0] r_b = {~e_b_zero, f_b};\n  wire [3:0]  lz_a, lz_b;\n  lzc11 u_lza (.r(r_a), .c(lz_a));\n  lzc11 u_lzb (.r(r_b), .c(lz_b));\n  wire [5:0] k_a = zero_a ? 6'd21 : {2'd0, lz_a};\n  wire [5:0] k_b = zero_b ? 6'd21 : {2'd0, lz_b};\n  wire [5:0] k   = k_a + k_b;\n\n  // effective exponent sum: subnormal operands contribute exponent 1\n  wire [5:0] J = {1'b0, e_a_zero ? 5'd1 : e_a} + {1'b0, e_b_zero ? 5'd1 : e_b};\n\n  wire [21:0] R = r_a * r_b;\n\n  // norm = (R << k)[21]  (product's leading one at bit 21 after normalization)\n  wire [32:0] Rp   = {11'b0, R};\n  wire        norm = Rp[32 - k[4:0]];\n\n  // W0 = J - k + norm = pre-rounding biased exponent + 15\n  wire signed [6:0] W0 = $signed({1'b0, J}) - $signed({1'b0, k}) + {6'd0, norm};\n\n  // --- unified window extraction ------------------------------------------\n  // u = window offset into R: 10-k+norm (normal result) or 26-J (subnormal result)\n  // u2 = u + 9;  w12 = R[u-1 +: 12]  ->  G = w12[0], window = w12[11:1]\n  wire sub_res = W0[6] | ~|W0[6:4];                    // W0 <= 15\n  wire [5:0] u2 = sub_res ? [ASSISTANT]
v7's critical path got worse (1294) — `sub_res` now sits in series with the extractor **select** (J → W0 → compare → mux → extract). The multiplier path is only ~1050ps. 

Fix (v8): two parallel window extracts with fast selects (`23-k` and `39-J`), then merge the window data with `sub_res` — keeping `sub_res` off both extract paths (the v6-style de-serialization, now with the cheap raw-product extractor):

[ASSISTANT]
v8's delay improved (1168) but the dual 32:1 extracts cost +11 area → ADP 123.8k, worse than v6. The dual-extract approach is a delay/area trade that doesn't pay.

Better: keep v6's compact structure and trim its tail. v9 = v6 + (a) bit-form overflow predicate (no wide comparator), (b) balanced prefix incrementer for the subnormal round sum, (c) flattened per-bit pack muxes:
  -> create_file(content="// fp_mul_e5f10 -- IEEE binary16 (1+5+10) multiplier, subnormals supported, RNE.\n// v9: v6 structure (pre-normalized operands, de-serialized subnormal shifter)\n// with flattened predicates, balanced incrementer, flat packing.\n\nmodule lzc10 (input [9:0] f, output [3:0] c);\n  wire [15:0] F  = {f, 6'b0};\n  wire        c3 = ~|F[15:8];\n  wire [7:0]  F2 = c3 ? F[7:0] : F[15:8];\n  wire        c2 = ~|F2[7:4];\n  wire [3:0]  F3 = c2 ? F2[3:0] : F2[7:4];\n  wire        c1 = ~|F3[3:2];\n  wire [1:0]  F4 = c1 ? F3[1:0] : F3[3:2];\n  wire        c0 = ~F4[1];\n  assign c = {c3, c2, c1, c0};\nendmodule\n\n// balanced carry-lookahead incrementer: S = A + Cin\nmodule inc10 (input [9:0] A, input Cin, output [9:0] S, output Cout);\n  wire [9:0] p = A;\n  wire [9:0] G [0:3];\n  wire [9:0] P [0:3];\n  assign G[0] = 10'd0;\n  assign P[0] = p;\n  genvar i, l;\n  generate\n    for (l = 1; l <= 3; l = l + 1) begin : lvl\n      for (i = 0; i < 10; i = i + 1) begin : b\n        if (i >= (1 << (l-1))) begin : hi\n          assign G[l][i] = G[l-1][i] | (P[l-1][i] & G[l-1][i - (1 << (l-1))]);\n          assign P[l][i] = P[l-1][i] & P[l-1][i - (1 << (l-1))];\n        end else begin : lo\n          assign G[l][i] = G[l-1][i];\n          assign P[l][i] = P[l-1][i];\n        end\n      end\n    end\n  endgenerate\n  wire [9:0] carry;\n  assign carry[0] = Cin;\n  generate\n    for (i = 1; i < 10; i = i + 1) begin : c\n      assign carry[i] = G[3][i-1] | (P[3][i-1] & Cin);\n    end\n  endgenerate\n  assign S = p ^ carry;\n  assign Cout = G[3][9] | (P[3][9] & Cin);\nendmodule\n\nmodule fp_mul_e5f10 (a, b, y);\n  input  [15:0] a;\n  input  [15:0] b;\n  output [15:0] y;\n\n  wire        s_a = a[15];\n  wire        s_b = b[15];\n  wire [4:0]  e_a = a[14:10];\n  wire [4:0]  e_b = b[14:10];\n  wire [9:0]  f_a = a[9:0];\n  wire [9:0]  f_b = b[9:0];\n\n  wire e_a_zero = (e_a == 5'd0);\n  wire e_b_zero = (e_b == 5'd0);\n  wire e_a_ones = (e_a == 5'd31);\n  wire e_b_ones = (e_b == 5'd31);\n  wire zero_a = e_a_zero & (f_a == 10'd0);\n  wire zero_b = e_b_zero & (f_b == 10'd0);\n  wire nan_a  = e_a_ones & (f_a != 10'd0);\n  wire nan_b  = e_b_ones & (f_b != 10'd0);\n  wire inf_a  = e_a_ones & (f_a == 10'd0);\n  wire inf_b  = e_b_ones & (f_b == 10'd0);\n\n  wire nan_in  = nan_a | nan_b | (inf_a & zero_b) | (zero_a & inf_b);\n  wire inf_in  = inf_a | inf_b;\n  wire s_y     = s_a ^ s_b;\n\n  wire [3:0] lzc_a, lzc_b;\n  lzc10 u_lzc_a (.f(f_a), .c(lzc_a));\n  lzc10 u_lzc_b (.f(f_b), .c(lzc_b));\n\n  // normalized significands (hidden one at bit 10) and signed exponents.\n  // Zero operands get a very negative exponent so the arithmetic path\n  // naturally produces a signed zero (product is 0 anyway).\n  wire [10:0] m_a = e_a_zero ? ({f_a, 1'b0} << lzc_a) : {1'b1, f_a};\n  wire [10:0] m_b = e_b_zero ? ({f_b, 1'b0} << lzc_b) : {1'b1, f_b};\n  wire signed [6:0] E_a = e_a_zero ? (zero_a ? -7'sd20 : -$signed({3'd0, lzc_a}))\n                                   : $signed({2'd0, e_a});\n  wire signed [6:0] E_b = e_b_zero ? (zero_b ? -7'sd20 : -$signed({3'd0, lzc_b}))\n                                   : $signed({2'd0, e_b});\n\n  wire [21:0] prod = m_a * m_b;\n  wire norm = prod[21];\n\n  // E_sum = E_a + E_b ; W0 = E_a + E_b + norm\n  wire signed [6:0] E_sum = E_a + E_b;\n  wire signed [6:0] W0    = E_sum + {6'd0, norm};\n\n  // below[j] = |prod[j:0], saturated above bit 21\n  wire [24:0] below;\n  genvar k;\n  generate\n    for (k = 0; k < 25; k = k + 1) begin : g_below\n      assign below[k] = (k < 22) ? |prod[k:0] : |prod[21:0];\n    end\n  endgenerate\n\n  // --- normal result path -------------------------------------------------\n  wire [10:0] mant_pre = norm ? prod[21:11] : prod[20:10];\n  wire        G_n      = norm ? prod[10] : prod[9];\n  wire        S_n      = norm ? below[9] : below[8];\n  wire        round_n  = G_n & (S_n | mant_pre[0]);\n  wire [11:0] mant_sum = {1'b0, mant_pre} + {11'd0, round_n};\n  wire        cy       = mant_sum[11];\n  wire [9:0]  frac_n   = cy ? mant_sum[10:1] : mant_sum[9:0];\n\n  // predicates from W0 = E_a + E_b + norm (pre-rounding biased exponent + 15)\n  wire sub_res = W0[6] | ~|W0[6:4];                    // W0 <= 15\n  wire w0_hi   = ~W0[6] & W0[5];\n  wire gt45    = w0_hi & (W0[4] | (W0[3] & W0[2] & W0[1]));\n  wire eq45    = w0_hi & ~W0[4] & W0[3] & W0[2] & ~W0[1] & W0[0];\n  wire ovf     = gt45 | (eq45 & cy);\n  wire [4:0] ey5 = W0[4:0] + (cy ? 5'd18 : 5'd17);     // W0 + cy - 15\n\n  // --- subnormal result path ---------------------------------------------\n  // value = prod * 2^(E_sum-50); subnormal frac = prod >> (26 - E_sum)\n  // 26 - E_sum = 11 + x  with  x = 15 - E_sum  (for E_sum in [1,15]).\n  // The shifter select uses only the low sum bits; the sign of E_sum forces\n  // a zero result in parallel with the shift (E_sum <= 0 => value tiny).\n  wire        zforce  = E_sum[6] | ~|E_sum[5:0];       // E_sum <= 0\n  wire [3:0]  x       = ~E_sum[3:0];\n  wire [15:0] w_ext   = {4'd0, prod[21:10]};           // prod >> 10\n  wire [15:0] w_sh    = w_ext >> ({1'b0, x} + 5'd1);\n  wire [9:0]  frac_t  = zforce ? 10'd0 : w_sh[9:0];\n  wire        G_s     = zforce ? 1'b0 : w_ext[x];\n  wire        S_s     = below[{1'b0, x} + 5'd9];\n  wire        round_s = G_s & (S_s | frac_t[0]);\n  wire [9:0]  frac_s;\n  wire        fs_co;\n  inc10 u_fs (.A(frac_t), .Cin(round_s), .S(frac_s), .Cout(fs_co));\n\n  // --- pack (flat) ---------------------------------------------------------\n  wire spec_e = nan_in | inf_in | ovf;    // exponent field 31\n  wire spec_f = inf_in | ovf;             // fraction field 0 (non-NaN specials)\n\n  wire [4:0] exp_y;\n  wire [9:0] frac_y;\n  assign exp_y[4:1] = {4{spec_e}} | ({4{~sub_res}} & ey5[4:1]);\n  assign exp_y[0]   = spec_e | (sub_res & fs_co) | (~sub_res & ey5[0]);\n  assign frac_y[8:0] = {9{~nan_in & ~spec_f}} &\n                       (sub_res ? frac_s[8:0] : frac_n[8:0]);\n  assign frac_y[9]  = nan_in | (~spec_f &\n                       (sub_res ? frac_s[9] : frac_n[9]));\n  wire sign_y = ~nan_in & s_y;\n\n  assign y = {sign_y, exp_y, frac_y};\nendmodule\n", filename='design_v9.sv')

[ASSISTANT]
v9's custom incrementer/flat-pack actually **hurt** (1326ps — ABC maps `+` and nested muxes better than my "flat" forms). Reverting those ideas.

Key insight for v10: v8's dual-extract is right structurally, but its extract selects (6-bit, chained after full-width adds) inflate both mux size and delay. Using 5-bit selects from **low sum bits only** (with R bottom-padded for the subnormal extract) shrinks the muxes to 24:1 and de-serializes the selects:
  -> create_file(content="// fp_mul_e5f10 -- IEEE binary16 (1+5+10) multiplier, subnormals supported, RNE.\n// v10: raw-significand multiply, two parallel window extracts with 5-bit fast\n// selects (low sum bits only); sub_res merges window data afterwards.\n\nmodule lzc11 (input [10:0] r, output [3:0] c);\n  wire [15:0] F  = {r, 5'b0};\n  wire        c3 = ~|F[15:8];\n  wire [7:0]  F2 = c3 ? F[7:0] : F[15:8];\n  wire        c2 = ~|F2[7:4];\n  wire [3:0]  F3 = c2 ? F2[3:0] : F2[7:4];\n  wire        c1 = ~|F3[3:2];\n  wire [1:0]  F4 = c1 ? F3[1:0] : F3[3:2];\n  wire        c0 = ~F4[1];\n  assign c = {c3, c2, c1, c0};\nendmodule\n\nmodule fp_mul_e5f10 (a, b, y);\n  input  [15:0] a;\n  input  [15:0] b;\n  output [15:0] y;\n\n  wire        s_a = a[15];\n  wire        s_b = b[15];\n  wire [4:0]  e_a = a[14:10];\n  wire [4:0]  e_b = b[14:10];\n  wire [9:0]  f_a = a[9:0];\n  wire [9:0]  f_b = b[9:0];\n\n  wire e_a_zero = (e_a == 5'd0);\n  wire e_b_zero = (e_b == 5'd0);\n  wire e_a_ones = (e_a == 5'd31);\n  wire e_b_ones = (e_b == 5'd31);\n  wire zero_a = e_a_zero & (f_a == 10'd0);\n  wire zero_b = e_b_zero & (f_b == 10'd0);\n  wire nan_a  = e_a_ones & (f_a != 10'd0);\n  wire nan_b  = e_b_ones & (f_b != 10'd0);\n  wire inf_a  = e_a_ones & (f_a == 10'd0);\n  wire inf_b  = e_b_ones & (f_b == 10'd0);\n\n  wire nan_in  = nan_a | nan_b | (inf_a & zero_b) | (zero_a & inf_b);\n  wire inf_in  = inf_a | inf_b;\n  wire s_y     = s_a ^ s_b;\n\n  // raw significands and their leading-zero counts (= pre-normalization shifts)\n  wire [10:0] r_a = {~e_a_zero, f_a};\n  wire [10:0] r_b = {~e_b_zero, f_b};\n  wire [3:0]  lz_a, lz_b;\n  lzc11 u_lza (.r(r_a), .c(lz_a));\n  lzc11 u_lzb (.r(r_b), .c(lz_b));\n  wire [5:0] k_a = zero_a ? 6'd21 : {2'd0, lz_a};\n  wire [5:0] k_b = zero_b ? 6'd21 : {2'd0, lz_b};\n  wire [5:0] k   = k_a + k_b;\n\n  // effective exponent sum: subnormal operands contribute exponent 1\n  wire [5:0] J = {1'b0, e_a_zero ? 5'd1 : e_a} + {1'b0, e_b_zero ? 5'd1 : e_b};\n\n  wire [21:0] R = r_a * r_b;\n\n  // norm = (R << k)[21]  (product's leading one at bit 21 after normalization)\n  wire [33:0] Rsh  = {R, 12'b0};\n  wire        norm = Rsh[33 - k[4:0]];\n\n  // W0 = J - k + norm = pre-rounding biased exponent + 15\n  wire signed [6:0] W0 = $signed({1'b0, J}) - $signed({1'b0, k}) + {6'd0, norm};\n  wire sub_res = W0[6] | ~|W0[6:4];                    // W0 <= 15\n\n  // --- two parallel window extracts (5-bit selects) -----------------------\n  // wA[i] = R[9-k+i]  (normal window R[20-k+norm:10-k+norm], G = R[9-k+norm])\n  // wB[i] = R[25-J+i] (subnormal frac R[35-J:26-J], G = R[25-J])\n  wire [63:0] T4 = {28'b0, R, 14'b0};                  // T4[m] = R[m-14]\n  wire [63:0] T5 = {42'b0, R};                         // T5[m] = R[m]\n  wire [4:0] uA = 5'd23 - k[4:0];\n  wire [4:0] uB = 5'd25 - J[4:0];\n  wire [12:0] wA = T4[uA +: 13];\n  wire [11:0] wB = T5[uB +: 12];\n\n  // Bq2[m] = |R[0 .. m-1]  (0 for m = 0, saturated above bit 21)\n  wire [63:0] Bq2;\n  genvar m;\n  generate\n    for (m = 0; m < 64; m = m + 1) begin : gB\n      if (m == 0)       assign Bq2[m] = 1'b0;\n      else if (m >= 23) assign Bq2[m] = |R;\n      else              assign Bq2[m] = |R[m-1:0];\n    end\n  endgenerate\n\n  wire [10:0] win_n = norm ? wA[12:2] : wA[11:1];\n  wire        G_n   = norm ? wA[1]   : wA[0];\n  wire        S_n   = norm ? Bq2[5'd10 - k[4:0]] : Bq2[5'd9 - k[4:0]];\n  wire [10:0] win_s = wB[11:1];      // top bit is 0 whenever sub_res holds\n  wire        G_s   = wB[0];\n  wire        S_s   = Bq2[uB + 5'd1];\n\n  // merge, then one rounding step (RNE)\n  wire [10:0] window = sub_res ? win_s : win_n;\n  wire        G      = sub_res ? G_s   : G_n;\n  wire        S      = sub_res ? S_s   : S_n;\n  wire        round  = G & (S | window[0]);\n  wire [11:0] sum12  = {1'b0, window} + {11'd0, round};\n  wire        cy     = sum12[11];          // normal-path carry\n  wire        fs_co  = sum12[10];          // subnormal carry (= exp LSB)\n  wire [9:0]  frac_n = cy ? sum12[10:1] : sum12[9:0];\n  wire [9:0]  frac_s = sum12[9:0];\n\n  // --- pack ---------------------------------------------------------------\n  wire ovf     = (W0 > 7'sd45) | ((W0 == 7'sd45) & cy);\n  wire [4:0] ey5 = W0[4:0] + (cy ? 5'd18 : 5'd17);     // W0 + cy - 15\n\n  wire spec_e = nan_in | inf_in | ovf;    // exponent field 31\n  wire spec_f = inf_in | ovf;             // fraction field 0 (non-NaN specials)\n\n  wire [4:0] exp_y  = spec_e ? 5'd31 :\n                      sub_res ? {4'd0, fs_co} : ey5;\n  wire [9:0] frac_y = nan_in  ? 10'h200 :\n                      spec_f  ? 10'd0 :\n                      sub_res ? frac_s : frac_n;\n  wire sign_y = nan_in ? 1'b0 : s_y;\n\n  assign y = {sign_y, exp_y, frac_y};\nendmodule\n", filename='design_v10.sv')

[ASSISTANT]
v10: ADP 116853 (delay 1151, area 101.5) — faster than v6 but bigger. To beat v6 I need v10's speed with less area. Since `k ≤ 10` whenever the normal window is used, the wA extract select fits in **4 bits** (16:1 muxes instead of 24:1), and the S_n indices too. Plus bit-form overflow. Let me build v11:
  -> create_file(content="// fp_mul_e5f10 -- IEEE binary16 (1+5+10) multiplier, subnormals supported, RNE.\n// v11: v10 with 4-bit normal-extract select (k <= 10 on that path), 4-bit S_n\n// indices, bit-form overflow predicate.\n\nmodule lzc11 (input [10:0] r, output [3:0] c);\n  wire [15:0] F  = {r, 5'b0};\n  wire        c3 = ~|F[15:8];\n  wire [7:0]  F2 = c3 ? [ASSISTANT]
v11 is worse (the 4-bit select didn't help). Back to v6's structure — its critical path starts with the **binary-search LZC** (~200ps of mux chain). A direct threshold-form LZC is much flatter. v12 = v6 + direct-form LZC + bit-form overflow:
  -> create_file(content="// fp_mul_e5f10 -- IEEE binary16 (1+5+10) multiplier, subnormals supported, RNE.\n// v12: v6 structure (pre-normalized operands, de-serialized subnormal shifter)\n// with direct-form LZC and bit-form overflow predicate.\n\nmodule lzc10 (input [9:0] f, output [3:0] c);\n  wire t1  = ~f[9];\n  wire t2  = ~|f[9:8];\n  wire t3  = ~|f[9:7];\n  wire t4  = ~|f[9:6];\n  wire t5  = ~|f[9:5];\n  wire t6  = ~|f[9:4];\n  wire t7  = ~|f[9:3];\n  wire t8  = ~|f[9:2];\n  wire t9  = ~|f[9:1];\n  wire t10 = ~|f[9:0];\n  assign c[3] = t8;\n  assign c[2] = t4 & ~t8;\n  assign c[1] = (t2 & ~t4) | (t6 & ~t8) | t10;\n  assign c[0] = (t1 & ~t2) | (t3 & ~t4) | (t5 & ~t6) | (t7 & ~t8) | (t9 & ~t10);\nendmodule\n\nmodule fp_mul_e5f10 (a, b, y);\n  input  [15:0] a;\n  input  [15:0] b;\n  output [15:0] y;\n\n  wire        s_a = a[15];\n  wire        s_b = b[15];\n  wire [4:0]  e_a = a[14:10];\n  wire [4:0]  e_b = b[14:10];\n  wire [9:0]  f_a = a[9:0];\n  wire [9:0]  f_b = b[9:0];\n\n  wire e_a_zero = (e_a == 5'd0);\n  wire e_b_zero = (e_b == 5'd0);\n  wire e_a_ones = (e_a == 5'd31);\n  wire e_b_ones = (e_b == 5'd31);\n  wire zero_a = e_a_zero & (f_a == 10'd0);\n  wire zero_b = e_b_zero & (f_b == 10'd0);\n  wire nan_a  = e_a_ones & (f_a != 10'd0);\n  wire nan_b  = e_b_ones & (f_b != 10'd0);\n  wire inf_a  = e_a_ones & (f_a == 10'd0);\n  wire inf_b  = e_b_ones & (f_b == 10'd0);\n\n  wire nan_in  = nan_a | nan_b | (inf_a & zero_b) | (zero_a & inf_b);\n  wire inf_in  = inf_a | inf_b;\n  wire s_y     = s_a ^ s_b;\n\n  wire [3:0] lzc_a, lzc_b;\n  lzc10 u_lzc_a (.f(f_a), .c(lzc_a));\n  lzc10 u_lzc_b (.f(f_b), .c(lzc_b));\n\n  // normalized significands (hidden one at bit 10) and signed exponents.\n  // Zero operands get a very negative exponent so the arithmetic path\n  // naturally produces a signed zero (product is 0 anyway).\n  wire [10:0] m_a = e_a_zero ? ({f_a, 1'b0} << lzc_a) : {1'b1, f_a};\n  wire [10:0] m_b = e_b_zero ? ({f_b, 1'b0} << lzc_b) : {1'b1, f_b};\n  wire signed [6:0] E_a = e_a_zero ? (zero_a ? -7'sd20 : -$signed({3'd0, lzc_a}))\n                                   : $signed({2'd0, e_a});\n  wire signed [6:0] E_b = e_b_zero ? (zero_b ? -7'sd20 : -$signed({3'd0, lzc_b}))\n                                   : $signed({2'd0, e_b});\n\n  wire [21:0] prod = m_a * m_b;\n  wire norm = prod[21];\n\n  // E_sum = E_a + E_b ; W0 = E_a + E_b + norm\n  wire signed [6:0] E_sum = E_a + E_b;\n  wire signed [6:0] W0    = E_sum + {6'd0, norm};\n\n  // below[j] = |prod[j:0], saturated above bit 21\n  wire [24:0] below;\n  genvar k;\n  generate\n    for (k = 0; k < 25; k = k + 1) begin : g_below\n      assign below[k] = (k < 22) ? |prod[k:0] : |prod[21:0];\n    end\n  endgenerate\n\n  // --- normal result path -------------------------------------------------\n  wire [10:0] mant_pre = norm ? prod[21:11] : prod[20:10];\n  wire        G_n      = norm ? prod[10] : prod[9];\n  wire        S_n      = norm ? below[9] : below[8];\n  wire        round_n  = G_n & (S_n | mant_pre[0]);\n  wire [11:0] mant_sum = {1'b0, mant_pre} + {11'd0, round_n};\n  wire        cy       = mant_sum[11];\n  wire [9:0]  frac_n   = cy ? mant_sum[10:1] : mant_sum[9:0];\n\n  // predicates from W0 = E_a + E_b + norm (pre-rounding biased exponent + 15)\n  wire sub_res = W0[6] | ~|W0[6:4];                    // W0 <= 15\n  wire w0_hi   = ~W0[6] & W0[5];\n  wire gt45    = w0_hi & (W0[4] | (W0[3] & W0[2] & W0[1]));\n  wire eq45    = w0_hi & ~W0[4] & W0[3] & W0[2] & ~W0[1] & W0[0];\n  wire ovf     = gt45 | (eq45 & cy);\n  wire [4:0] ey5 = W0[4:0] + (cy ? 5'd18 : 5'd17);     // W0 + cy - 15\n\n  // --- subnormal result path ---------------------------------------------\n  // value = prod * 2^(E_sum-50); subnormal frac = prod >> (26 - E_sum)\n  // 26 - E_sum = 11 + x  with  x = 15 - E_sum  (for E_sum in [1,15]).\n  // The shifter select uses only the low sum bits; the sign of E_sum forces\n  // a zero result in parallel with the shift (E_sum <= 0 => value tiny).\n  wire        zforce  = E_sum[6] | ~|E_sum[5:0];       // E_sum <= 0\n  wire [3:0]  x       = ~E_sum[3:0];\n  wire [15:0] w_ext   = {4'd0, prod[21:10]};           // prod >> 10\n  wire [15:0] w_sh    = w_ext >> ({1'b0, x} + 5'd1);\n  wire [9:0]  frac_t  = zforce ? 10'd0 : w_sh[9:0];\n  wire        G_s     = zforce ? 1'b0 : w_ext[x];\n  wire        S_s     = below[{1'b0, x} + 5'd9];\n  wire        round_s = G_s & (S_s | frac_t[0]);\n  wire [10:0] frac_sum = {1'b0, frac_t} + {10'd0, round_s};\n  wire        fs_co    = frac_sum[10];\n\n  // --- pack ---------------------------------------------------------------\n  wire spec_e = nan_in | inf_in | ovf;    // exponent field 31\n  wire spec_f = inf_in | ovf;             // fraction field 0 (non-NaN specials)\n\n  wire [4:0] exp_y  = spec_e ? 5'd31 :\n                      sub_res ? {4'd0, fs_co} : ey5;\n  wire [9:0] frac_y = nan_in  ? 10'h200 :\n                      spec_f  ? 10'd0 :\n                      sub_res ? frac_sum[9:0] : frac_n;\n  wire sign_y = nan_in ? 1'b0 : s_y;\n\n  assign y = {sign_y, exp_y, frac_y};\nendmodule\n", filename='design_v12.sv')

[ASSISTANT]
v12 is a new best: ADP 111506 (delay 1196, area 93.2). 

One cheap de-serialization remains in the sub shifter: `w_ext >> (x+1)` requires a **5-bit select incrementer** (x+1) before the mux decoder. Splitting it as `(w_ext >> 1) >> x` makes the fixed shift pure wiring and the select plain `x`:

[ASSISTANT]
v13 is identical to v12 (ABC already folds the constant). The real remaining depth is the **S_s extraction** (`below[x+9]` = a 25:1 mux) on the rounding path. 

Classic trick: shift a vector `{w_ext, prefixOR(w_ext), 0}` together — then the window, G, and S all land at **fixed positions** (no wide sticky mux). Let me apply it (v14):
  -> create_file(content="// fp_mul_e5f10 -- IEEE binary16 (1+5+10) multiplier, subnormals supported, RNE.\n// v14: v13 with joint window/sticky extraction: shifting {w_ext, prefixOR, 0}\n// places frac/G/S at fixed positions, removing the wide sticky mux.\n\nmodule lzc10 (input [9:0] f, output [3:0] c);\n  wire t1  = ~f[9];\n  wire t2  = ~|f[9:8];\n  wire t3  = ~|f[9:7];\n  wire t4  = ~|f[9:6];\n  wire t5  = ~|f[9:5];\n  wire t6  = ~|f[9:4];\n  wire t7  = ~|f[9:3];\n  wire t8  = ~|f[9:2];\n  wire t9  = ~|f[9:1];\n  wire t10 = ~|f[9:0];\n  assign c[3] = t8;\n  assign c[2] = t4 & ~t8;\n  assign c[1] = (t2 & ~t4) | (t6 & ~t8) | t10;\n  assign c[0] = (t1 & ~t2) | (t3 & ~t4) | (t5 & ~t6) | (t7 & ~t8) | (t9 & ~t10);\nendmodule\n\nmodule fp_mul_e5f10 (a, b, y);\n  input  [15:0] a;\n  input  [15:0] b;\n  output [15:0] y;\n\n  wire        s_a = a[15];\n  wire        s_b = b[15];\n  wire [4:0]  e_a = a[14:10];\n  wire [4:0]  e_b = b[14:10];\n  wire [9:0]  f_a = a[9:0];\n  wire [9:0]  f_b = b[9:0];\n\n  wire e_a_zero = (e_a == 5'd0);\n  wire e_b_zero = (e_b == 5'd0);\n  wire e_a_ones = (e_a == 5'd31);\n  wire e_b_ones = (e_b == 5'd31);\n  wire zero_a = e_a_zero & (f_a == 10'd0);\n  wire zero_b = e_b_zero & (f_b == 10'd0);\n  wire nan_a  = e_a_ones & (f_a != 10'd0);\n  wire nan_b  = e_b_ones & (f_b != 10'd0);\n  wire inf_a  = e_a_ones & (f_a == 10'd0);\n  wire inf_b  = e_b_ones & (f_b == 10'd0);\n\n  wire nan_in  = nan_a | nan_b | (inf_a & zero_b) | (zero_a & inf_b);\n  wire inf_in  = inf_a | inf_b;\n  wire s_y     = s_a ^ s_b;\n\n  wire [3:0] lzc_a, lzc_b;\n  lzc10 u_lzc_a (.f(f_a), .c(lzc_a));\n  lzc10 u_lzc_b (.f(f_b), .c(lzc_b));\n\n  // normalized significands (hidden one at bit 10) and signed exponents.\n  // Zero operands get a very negative exponent so the arithmetic path\n  // naturally produces a signed zero (product is 0 anyway).\n  wire [10:0] m_a = e_a_zero ? ({f_a, 1'b0} << lzc_a) : {1'b1, f_a};\n  wire [10:0] m_b = e_b_zero ? ({f_b, 1'b0} << lzc_b) : {1'b1, f_b};\n  wire signed [6:0] E_a = e_a_zero ? (zero_a ? -7'sd20 : -$signed({3'd0, lzc_a}))\n                                   : $signed({2'd0, e_a});\n  wire signed [6:0] E_b = e_b_zero ? (zero_b ? -7'sd20 : -$signed({3'd0, lzc_b}))\n                                   : $signed({2'd0, e_b});\n\n  wire [21:0] prod = m_a * m_b;\n  wire norm = prod[21];\n\n  // E_sum = E_a + E_b ; W0 = E_a + E_b + norm\n  wire signed [6:0] E_sum = E_a + E_b;\n  wire signed [6:0] W0    = E_sum + {6'd0, norm};\n\n  // --- normal result path -------------------------------------------------\n  wire [10:0] mant_pre = norm ? prod[21:11] : prod[20:10];\n  wire        G_n      = norm ? prod[10] : prod[9];\n  wire        S_n      = norm ? (|prod[9:0]) : (|prod[8:0]);\n  wire        round_n  = G_n & (S_n | mant_pre[0]);\n  wire [11:0] mant_sum = {1'b0, mant_pre} + {11'd0, round_n};\n  wire        cy       = mant_sum[11];\n  wire [9:0]  frac_n   = cy ? mant_sum[10:1] : mant_sum[9:0];\n\n  // predicates from W0 = E_a + E_b + norm (pre-rounding biased exponent + 15)\n  wire sub_res = W0[6] | ~|W0[6:4];                    // W0 <= 15\n  wire w0_hi   = ~W0[6] & W0[5];\n  wire gt45    = w0_hi & (W0[4] | (W0[3] & W0[2] & W0[1]));\n  wire eq45    = w0_hi & ~W0[4] & W0[3] & W0[2] & ~W0[1] & W0[0];\n  wire ovf     = gt45 | (eq45 & cy);\n  wire [4:0] ey5 = W0[4:0] + (cy ? 5'd18 : 5'd17);     // W0 + cy - 15\n\n  // --- subnormal result path ---------------------------------------------\n  // frac = prod >> (26 - E_sum) = (prod >> 11) >> x,  x = 15 - E_sum.\n  // Joint extraction: shift {w_ext, prefixOR(w_ext), 1'b0} by x so that frac,\n  // G and S land at fixed positions (no wide sticky mux).\n  wire [15:0] w_ext = {4'd0, prod[21:10]};             // prod >> 10\n  wire [15:0] bext;                                    // bext[j] = |w_ext[j-1:0]\n  genvar j;\n  generate\n    for (j = 0; j < 16; j = j + 1) begin : gB\n      if (j == 0) assign bext[j] = 1'b0;\n      else        assign bext[j] = |w_ext[j-1:0];\n    end\n  endgenerate\n  wire [32:0] Pv = {w_ext, bext, 1'b0};\n  wire [32:0] Q  = Pv >> {1'b0, ~E_sum[3:0], 1'b0} >> 1'b0;   // shift by x\n  wire        zforce = E_sum[6] | ~|E_sum[5:0];        // E_sum <= 0\n  wire [9:0]  frac_t = zforce ? 10'd0 : Q[27:18];\n  wire        G_s    = zforce ? 1'b0 : Q[17];\n  wire        S_s    = Q[1];\n  wire        round_s = G_s & (S_s | frac_t[0]);\n  wire [10:0] frac_sum = {1'b0, frac_t} + {10'd0, round_s};\n  wire        fs_co    = frac_sum[10];\n\n  // --- pack ---------------------------------------------------------------\n  wire spec_e = nan_in | inf_in | ovf;    // exponent field 31\n  wire spec_f = inf_in | ovf;             // fraction field 0 (non-NaN specials)\n\n  wire [4:0] exp_y  = spec_e ? 5'd31 :\n                      sub_res ? {4'd0, fs_co} : ey5;\n  wire [9:0] frac_y = nan_in  ? 10'h200 :\n                      spec_f  ? 10'd0 :\n                      sub_res ? frac_sum[9:0] : frac_n;\n  wire sign_y = nan_in ? 1'b0 : s_y;\n\n  assign y = {sign_y, exp_y, frac_y};\nendmodule\n", filename='design_v14.sv')

v14 is a new best: ADP 108672 (delay 1147). The critical path is now the **exponent chain**: mult → `norm` → `W0` adder → `ey5` adder → pack muxes (3 chained adders). 

Key simplification: define `E_y = E_sum + C(norm, cy)` directly (one adder, selected constant −13/−14/−15). Then `sub_res = (E_y ≤ cy)` and `ovf = ~E_y[6] & (E_y[5] | &E_y[4:0])` — removing an entire adder stage:
  -> create_file(content="// fp_mul_e5f10 -- IEEE binary16 (1+5+10) multiplier, subnormals supported, RNE.\n// v15: v14 with folded exponent: E_y = E_sum + C(norm,cy) directly; sub_res and\n// overflow are shallow bit-forms of E_y (one adder less on the exponent path).\n\nmodule lzc10 (input [9:0] f, output [3:0] c);\n  wire t1  = ~f[9];\n  wire t2  = ~|f[9:8];\n  wire t3  = ~|f[9:7];\n  wire t4  = ~|f[9:6];\n  wire t5  = ~|f[9:5];\n  wire t6  = ~|f[9:4];\n  wire t7  = ~|f[9:3];\n  wire t8  = ~|f[9:2];\n  wire t9  = ~|f[9:1];\n  wire t10 = ~|f[9:0];\n  assign c[3] = t8;\n  assign c[2] = t4 & ~t8;\n  assign c[1] = (t2 & ~t4) | (t6 & ~t8) | t10;\n  assign c[0] = (t1 & ~t2) | (t3 & ~t4) | (t5 & ~t6) | (t7 & ~t8) | (t9 & ~t10);\nendmodule\n\nmodule fp_mul_e5f10 (a, b, y);\n  input  [15:0] a;\n  input  [15:0] b;\n  output [15:0] y;\n\n  wire        s_a = a[15];\n  wire        s_b = b[15];\n  wire [4:0]  e_a = a[14:10];\n  wire [4:0]  e_b = b[14:10];\n  wire [9:0]  f_a = a[9:0];\n  wire [9:0]  f_b = b[9:0];\n\n  wire e_a_zero = (e_a == 5'd0);\n  wire e_b_zero = (e_b == 5'd0);\n  wire e_a_ones = (e_a == 5'd31);\n  wire e_b_ones = (e_b == 5'd31);\n  wire zero_a = e_a_zero & (f_a == 10'd0);\n  wire zero_b = e_b_zero & (f_b == 10'd0);\n  wire nan_a  = e_a_ones & (f_a != 10'd0);\n  wire nan_b  = e_b_ones & (f_b != 10'd0);\n  wire inf_a  = e_a_ones & (f_a == 10'd0);\n  wire inf_b  = e_b_ones & (f_b == 10'd0);\n\n  wire nan_in  = nan_a | nan_b | (inf_a & zero_b) | (zero_a & inf_b);\n  wire inf_in  = inf_a | inf_b;\n  wire s_y     = s_a ^ s_b;\n\n  wire [3:0] lzc_a, lzc_b;\n  lzc10 u_lzc_a (.f(f_a), .c(lzc_a));\n  lzc10 u_lzc_b (.f(f_b), .c(lzc_b));\n\n  // normalized significands (hidden one at bit 10) and signed exponents.\n  // Zero operands get a very negative exponent so the arithmetic path\n  // naturally produces a signed zero (product is 0 anyway).\n  wire [10:0] m_a = e_a_zero ? (|prod[9:0]) : (|prod[8:0]);\n  wire        round_n  = G_n & (S_n | mant_pre[0]);\n  wire [11:0] mant_sum = {1'b0, mant_pre} + {11'd0, round_n};\n  wire        cy       = mant_sum[11];\n  wire [9:0]  frac_n   = cy ? mant_sum[10:1] : mant_sum[9:0];\n\n  // exponent of a normal result: E_y = E_sum + norm + cy - 15\n  wire signed [6:0] C  = cy ? (E_y[6] | ~|E_y[6:1]) : (E_y[6] | ~|E_y[5:0]);\n  wire sub_res = le_cy;\n  wire ovf = ~E_y[6] & (E_y[5] | &E_y[4:0]);\n  wire [4:0] ey5 = E_y[4:0];\n\n  // --- subnormal result path ---------------------------------------------\n  // frac = prod >> (26 - E_sum) = (prod >> 11) >> x,  x = 15 - E_sum.\n  // Joint extraction: shift {w_ext, prefixOR, 1'b0} by x so that frac, G and S\n  // land at fixed positions (no wide sticky mux).\n  wire [15:0] w_ext = {4'd0, prod[21:10]};             // prod >> 10\n  wire        S0    = |prod[9:0];                      // bits below w_ext's base\n  wire [15:0] bext;                                    // bext[j] = |prod[0..9+j]\n  genvar j;\n  generate\n    for (j = 0; j < 16; j = j + 1) begin : gB\n      if (j == 0) assign bext[j] = S0;\n      else        assign bext[j] = S0 | |w_ext[j-1:0];\n    end\n  endgenerate\n  wire [32:0] Pv = {w_ext, bext, 1'b0};\n  wire [3:0]  x  = ~E_sum[3:0];\n  wire [32:0] Q  = Pv >> x;                            // shift by x\n  wire        zforce = E_sum[6] | ~|E_sum[5:0];        // E_sum <= 0\n  wire [9:0]  frac_t = zforce ? 10'd0 : Q[27:18];\n  wire        G_s    = zforce ? 1'b0 : Q[17];\n  wire        S_s    = Q[1];\n  wire        round_s = G_s & (S_s | frac_t[0]);\n  wire [10:0] frac_sum = {1'b0, frac_t} + {10'd0, round_s};\n  wire        fs_co    = frac_sum[10];\n\n  // --- pack ---------------------------------------------------------------\n  wire spec_e = nan_in | inf_in | ovf;    // exponent field 31\n  wire spec_f = inf_in | ovf;             // fraction field 0 (non-NaN specials)\n\n  wire [4:0] exp_y  = spec_e ? 5'd31 :\n                      sub_res ? {4'd0, fs_co} : ey5;\n  wire [9:0] frac_y = nan_in  ? 10'h200 :\n                      spec_f  ? 10'd0 :\n                      sub_res ? frac_sum[9:0] : frac_n;\n  wire sign_y = nan_in ? 1'b0 : s_y;\n\n  assign y = {sign_y, exp_y, frac_y};\nendmodule\n", filename='design_v15.sv')
