[ASSISTANT]
Identical netlist — target_delay is robust here. 

One last high-value idea for v14: `ey5 = W0[4:0] + (cy?18:17)` is chained after the 7-bit `W0` adder. But `ey5` only needs the **low 5 bits** of the exponent sum, which depend only on `E_sum[4:0]` (no carry-in!). So `ey5 = E_sum[4:0] + K(norm,cy)` — one shallow 5-bit add in parallel with `W0`:
  -> create_file(content="// fp_mul_e5f10 -- IEEE binary16 (1+5+10) multiplier, subnormals supported, RNE.\n// v19: v14 with shallow exponent output: ey5 = E_sum[4:0] + K(norm,cy)\n// computed in parallel with W0 (low sum bits need no carry-in).\n\nmodule lzc10 (input [9:0] f, output [3:0] c);\n  wire t1  = ~f[9];\n  wire t2  = ~|f[9:8];\n  wire t3  = ~|f[9:7];\n  wire t4  = ~|f[9:6];\n  wire t5  = ~|f[9:5];\n  wire t6  = ~|f[9:4];\n  wire t7  = ~|f[9:3];\n  wire t8  = ~|f[9:2];\n  wire t9  = ~|f[9:1];\n  wire t10 = ~|f[9:0];\n  assign c[3] = t8;\n  assign c[2] = t4 & ~t8;\n  assign c[1] = (t2 & ~t4) | (t6 & ~t8) | t10;\n  assign c[0] = (t1 & ~t2) | (t3 & ~t4) | (t5 & ~t6) | (t7 & ~t8) | (t9 & ~t10);\nendmodule\n\nmodule fp_mul_e5f10 (a, b, y);\n  input  [15:0] a;\n  input  [15:0] b;\n  output [15:0] y;\n\n  wire        s_a = a[15];\n  wire        s_b = b[15];\n  wire [4:0]  e_a = a[14:10];\n  wire [4:0]  e_b = b[14:10];\n  wire [9:0]  f_a = a[9:0];\n  wire [9:0]  f_b = b[9:0];\n\n  wire e_a_zero = (e_a == 5'd0);\n  wire e_b_zero = (e_b == 5'd0);\n  wire e_a_ones = (e_a == 5'd31);\n  wire e_b_ones = (e_b == 5'd31);\n  wire zero_a = e_a_zero & (f_a == 10'd0);\n  wire zero_b = e_b_zero & (f_b == 10'd0);\n  wire nan_a  = e_a_ones & (f_a != 10'd0);\n  wire nan_b  = e_b_ones & (f_b != 10'd0);\n  wire inf_a  = e_a_ones & (f_a == 10'd0);\n  wire inf_b  = e_b_ones & (f_b == 10'd0);\n\n  wire nan_in  = nan_a | nan_b | (inf_a & zero_b) | (zero_a & inf_b);\n  wire inf_in  = inf_a | inf_b;\n  wire s_y     = s_a ^ s_b;\n\n  wire [3:0] lzc_a, lzc_b;\n  lzc10 u_lzc_a (.f(f_a), .c(lzc_a));\n  lzc10 u_lzc_b (.f(f_b), .c(lzc_b));\n\n  // normalized significands (hidden one at bit 10) and signed exponents.\n  // Zero operands get a very negative exponent so the arithmetic path\n  // naturally produces a signed zero (product is 0 anyway).\n  wire [10:0] m_a = e_a_zero ? ({f_a, 1'b0} << lzc_a) : {1'b1, f_a};\n  wire [10:0] m_b = e_b_zero ? ({f_b, 1'b0} << lzc_b) : {1'b1, f_b};\n  wire signed [6:0] E_a = e_a_zero ? (zero_a ? -7'sd20 : -$signed({3'd0, lzc_a}))\n                                   : $signed({2'd0, e_a});\n  wire signed [6:0] E_b = e_b_zero ? (zero_b ? -7'sd20 : -$signed({3'd0, lzc_b}))\n                                   : $signed({2'd0, e_b});\n\n  wire [21:0] prod = m_a * m_b;\n  wire norm = prod[21];\n\n  // E_sum = E_a + E_b ; W0 = E_a + E_b + norm\n  wire signed [6:0] E_sum = E_a + E_b;\n  wire signed [6:0] W0    = E_sum + {6'd0, norm};\n\n  // --- normal result path -------------------------------------------------\n  wire [10:0] mant_pre = norm ? prod[21:11] : prod[20:10];\n  wire        G_n      = norm ? prod[10] : prod[9];\n  wire        S_n      = norm ? (|prod[9:0]) : (|prod[8:0]);\n  wire        round_n  = G_n & (S_n | mant_pre[0]);\n  wire [11:0] mant_sum = {1'b0, mant_pre} + {11'd0, round_n};\n  wire        cy       = mant_sum[11];\n  wire [9:0]  frac_n   = cy ? mant_sum[10:1] : mant_sum[9:0];\n\n  // predicates from W0 = E_a + E_b + norm (pre-rounding biased exponent + 15)\n  wire sub_res = W0[6] | ~|W0[6:4];                    // W0 <= 15\n  wire w0_hi   = ~W0[6] & W0[5];\n  wire gt45    = w0_hi & (W0[4] | (W0[3] & W0[2] & W0[1]));\n  wire eq45    = w0_hi & ~W0[4] & W0[3] & W0[2] & ~W0[1] & W0[0];\n  wire ovf     = gt45 | (eq45 & cy);\n  // low 5 bits of the normal exponent: E_sum + norm + cy - 15 (mod 32)\n  wire [4:0] K   = cy ? (norm ? 5'd19 : 5'd18) : (norm ? 5'd18 : 5'd17);\n  wire [4:0] ey5 = E_sum[4:0] + K;\n\n  // --- subnormal result path ---------------------------------------------\n  // frac = prod >> (26 - E_sum) = (prod >> 11) >> x,  x = 15 - E_sum.\n  // Joint extraction: shift {w_ext, prefixOR, 1'b0} by x so that frac, G and S\n  // land at fixed positions (no wide sticky mux).\n  wire [15:0] w_ext = {4'd0, prod[21:10]};             // prod >> 10\n  wire        S0    = |prod[9:0];                      // bits below w_ext's base\n  wire [15:0] bext;                                    // bext[j] = |prod[0..9+j]\n  genvar j;\n  generate\n    for (j = 0; j < 16; j = j + 1) begin : gB\n      if (j == 0) assign bext[j] = S0;\n      else        assign bext[j] = S0 | |w_ext[j-1:0];\n    end\n  endgenerate\n  wire [32:0] Pv = {w_ext, bext, 1'b0};\n  wire [3:0]  x  = ~E_sum[3:0];\n  wire [32:0] Q  = Pv >> x;                            // shift by x\n  wire        zforce = E_sum[6] | ~|E_sum[5:0];        // E_sum <= 0\n  wire [9:0]  frac_t = zforce ? 10'd0 : Q[27:18];\n  wire        G_s    = zforce ? 1'b0 : Q[17];\n  wire        S_s    = Q[1];\n  wire        round_s = G_s & (S_s | frac_t[0]);\n  wire [10:0] frac_sum = {1'b0, frac_t} + {10'd0, round_s};\n  wire        fs_co    = frac_sum[10];\n\n  // --- pack ---------------------------------------------------------------\n  wire spec_e = nan_in | inf_in | ovf;    // exponent field 31\n  wire spec_f = inf_in | ovf;             // fraction field 0 (non-NaN specials)\n\n  wire [4:0] exp_y  = spec_e ? 5'd31 :\n                      sub_res ? {4'd0, fs_co} : ey5;\n  wire [9:0] frac_y = nan_in  ? 10'h200 :\n                      spec_f  ? 10'd0 :\n                      sub_res ? frac_sum[9:0] : frac_n;\n  wire sign_y = nan_in ? 1'b0 : s_y;\n\n  assign y = {sign_y, exp_y, frac_y};\nendmodule\n", filename='design_v19.sv')

            -1203.29   data arrival time

[ASSISTANT]
v19 also regressed — v14's mapping is the sweet spot. With the remaining steps, let me make the best design canonical in `design.sv` and confirm it once more:
  -> create_file(content="// fp_mul_e5f10 -- IEEE binary16 (1+5+10) multiplier, subnormals supported, RNE.\n// Final design (v14): pre-normalized operands (LZC + 11-bit shift), product in\n// [2^20,2^22), 2:1 normal mantissa select, de-serialized subnormal shifter with\n// joint window/sticky extraction (shift {w_ext, prefixOR, 0} places frac/G/S at\n// fixed positions), single rounding step, compact packing.\n// Zero operands use an exaggerated exponent so the datapath yields a signed\n// zero naturally. Bit-exact IEEE: RNE incl. subnormal results, NaN -> 0x7E00,\n// inf*0 -> NaN, overflow -> inf, zero -> signed zero.\n\nmodule lzc10 (input [9:0] f, output [3:0] c);\n  wire t1  = ~f[9];\n  wire t2  = ~|f[9:8];\n  wire t3  = ~|f[9:7];\n  wire t4  = ~|f[9:6];\n  wire t5  = ~|f[9:5];\n  wire t6  = ~|f[9:4];\n  wire t7  = ~|f[9:3];\n  wire t8  = ~|f[9:2];\n  wire t9  = ~|f[9:1];\n  wire t10 = ~|f[9:0];\n  assign c[3] = t8;\n  assign c[2] = t4 & ~t8;\n  assign c[1] = (t2 & ~t4) | (t6 & ~t8) | t10;\n  assign c[0] = (t1 & ~t2) | (t3 & ~t4) | (t5 & ~t6) | (t7 & ~t8) | (t9 & ~t10);\nendmodule\n\nmodule fp_mul_e5f10 (a, b, y);\n  input  [15:0] a;\n  input  [15:0] b;\n  output [15:0] y;\n\n  wire        s_a = a[15];\n  wire        s_b = b[15];\n  wire [4:0]  e_a = a[14:10];\n  wire [4:0]  e_b = b[14:10];\n  wire [9:0]  f_a = a[9:0];\n  wire [9:0]  f_b = b[9:0];\n\n  wire e_a_zero = (e_a == 5'd0);\n  wire e_b_zero = (e_b == 5'd0);\n  wire e_a_ones = (e_a == 5'd31);\n  wire e_b_ones = (e_b == 5'd31);\n  wire zero_a = e_a_zero & (f_a == 10'd0);\n  wire zero_b = e_b_zero & (f_b == 10'd0);\n  wire nan_a  = e_a_ones & (f_a != 10'd0);\n  wire nan_b  = e_b_ones & (f_b != 10'd0);\n  wire inf_a  = e_a_ones & (f_a == 10'd0);\n  wire inf_b  = e_b_ones & (f_b == 10'd0);\n\n  wire nan_in  = nan_a | nan_b | (inf_a & zero_b) | (zero_a & inf_b);\n  wire inf_in  = inf_a | inf_b;\n  wire s_y     = s_a ^ s_b;\n\n  wire [3:0] lzc_a, lzc_b;\n  lzc10 u_lzc_a (.f(f_a), .c(lzc_a));\n  lzc10 u_lzc_b (.f(f_b), .c(lzc_b));\n\n  // normalized significands (hidden one at bit 10) and signed exponents.\n  // Zero operands get a very negative exponent so the arithmetic path\n  // naturally produces a signed zero (product is 0 anyway).\n  wire [10:0] m_a = e_a_zero ? ({f_a, 1'b0} << lzc_a) : {1'b1, f_a};\n  wire [10:0] m_b = e_b_zero ? ({f_b, 1'b0} << lzc_b) : {1'b1, f_b};\n  wire signed [6:0] E_a = e_a_zero ? (zero_a ? -7'sd20 : -$signed({3'd0, lzc_a}))\n                                   : $signed({2'd0, e_a});\n  wire signed [6:0] E_b = e_b_zero ? (zero_b ? -7'sd20 : -$signed({3'd0, lzc_b}))\n                                   : $signed({2'd0, e_b});\n\n  wire [21:0] prod = m_a * m_b;\n  wire norm = prod[21];\n\n  // E_sum = E_a + E_b ; W0 = E_a + E_b + norm\n  wire signed [6:0] E_sum = E_a + E_b;\n  wire signed [6:0] W0    = E_sum + {6'd0, norm};\n\n  // --- normal result path -------------------------------------------------\n  wire [10:0] mant_pre = norm ? prod[21:11] : prod[20:10];\n  wire        G_n      = norm ? prod[10] : prod[9];\n  wire        S_n      = norm ? (|prod[9:0]) : (|prod[8:0]);\n  wire        round_n  = G_n & (S_n | mant_pre[0]);\n  wire [11:0] mant_sum = {1'b0, mant_pre} + {11'd0, round_n};\n  wire        cy       = mant_sum[11];\n  wire [9:0]  frac_n   = cy ? mant_sum[10:1] : mant_sum[9:0];\n\n  // predicates from W0 = E_a + E_b + norm (pre-rounding biased exponent + 15)\n  wire sub_res = W0[6] | ~|W0[6:4];                    // W0 <= 15\n  wire w0_hi   = ~W0[6] & W0[5];\n  wire gt45    = w0_hi & (W0[4] | (W0[3] & W0[2] & W0[1]));\n  wire eq45    = w0_hi & ~W0[4] & W0[3] & W0[2] & ~W0[1] & W0[0];\n  wire ovf     = gt45 | (eq45 & cy);\n  wire [4:0] ey5 = W0[4:0] + (cy ? 5'd18 : 5'd17);     // W0 + cy - 15\n\n  // --- subnormal result path ---------------------------------------------\n  // frac = prod >> (26 - E_sum) = (prod >> 11) >> x,  x = 15 - E_sum.\n  // Joint extraction: shift {w_ext, prefixOR, 1'b0} by x so that frac, G and S\n  // land at fixed positions (no wide sticky mux).\n  wire [15:0] w_ext = {4'd0, prod[21:10]};             // prod >> 10\n  wire        S0    = |prod[9:0];                      // bits below w_ext's base\n  wire [15:0] bext;                                    // bext[j] = |prod[0..9+j]\n  genvar j;\n  generate\n    for (j = 0; j < 16; j = j + 1) begin : gB\n      if (j == 0) assign bext[j] = S0;\n      else        assign bext[j] = S0 | |w_ext[j-1:0];\n    end\n  endgenerate\n  wire [32:0] Pv = {w_ext, bext, 1'b0};\n  wire [3:0]  x  = ~E_sum[3:0];\n  wire [32:0] Q  = Pv >> x;                            // shift by x\n  wire        zforce = E_sum[6] | ~|E_sum[5:0];        // E_sum <= 0\n  wire [9:0]  frac_t = zforce ? 10'd0 : Q[27:18];\n  wire        G_s    = zforce ? 1'b0 : Q[17];\n  wire        S_s    = Q[1];\n  wire        round_s = G_s & (S_s | frac_t[0]);\n  wire [10:0] frac_sum = {1'b0, frac_t} + {10'd0, round_s};\n  wire        fs_co    = frac_sum[10];\n\n  // --- pack ---------------------------------------------------------------\n  wire spec_e = nan_in | inf_in | ovf;    // exponent field 31\n  wire spec_f = inf_in | ovf;             // fraction field 0 (non-NaN specials)\n\n  wire [4:0] exp_y  = spec_e ? 5'd31 :\n                      sub_res ? {4'd0, fs_co} : ey5;\n  wire [9:0] frac_y = nan_in  ? 10'h200 :\n                      spec_f  ? 10'd0 :\n                      sub_res ? frac_sum[9:0] : frac_n;\n  wire sign_y = nan_in ? 1'b0 : s_y;\n\n  assign y = {sign_y, exp_y, frac_y};\nendmodule\n", filename='design.sv')

[ASSISTANT]
The final design is confirmed. Here's a summary of the optimization journey:

## Results

| Variant | Key idea | Area | Delay (ps) | **ADP** |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| starting_point | unpack/mul/normalize/round/pack | 108.7 | 1338 | 145429 |
| v2 | pre-normalize subnormal inputs (LZC + small shift) | 94.2 | 1384 | 130381 |
| v3 | flat pack, auto-zero via exaggerated exponent | 93.5 | 1319 | 123349 |
| v6 | de-serialized shifter select (low sum bits + parallel sign) | 94.5 | 1201 | 113515 |
| v12 | direct-form LZC + bit-form overflow | 93.2 | 1196 | 111506 |
| **v14 (final)** | **joint window/sticky extraction** | **94.7** | **1147** | **108672** |

**25% improvement** over the starting point, with 100% correctness on all 18,874,371 test vectors.

## Final design (`design.sv`) — key techniques
1. **Input pre-normalization**: subnormal operands are shifted (LZC-driven) so the product always lies in [2²⁰, 2²²) — the normal result needs only a 2:1 mantissa select (no product-side LZC, no wide normalize shifter).
2. **De-serialized subnormal path**: the shifter select `x = ~E_sum[3:0]` uses only low sum bits (no carry chain), while the slow sign bit forces a zero result *in parallel* with the shift.
3. **Joint window/sticky extraction**: shifting `{w_ext, prefixOR(w_ext), 1'b0}` by `x` places fraction, guard bit, **and sticky bit** at fixed positions — eliminating the 25:1 sticky mux.
4. **Single rounding step** with the subnormal carry becoming the exponent LSB directly (`y_sub = {s, 4'b0, frac_sum}`).
5. **Zero-result trick**: zero operands get a hugely negative exponent so the datapath produces the signed zero naturally (one less pack mux).
6. Direct-form threshold LZC and bit-form overflow/underflow predicates.

Notable negative results (reverted): custom prefix incrementers, "flat" per-bit pack formulas, and folded exponent adders all caused ABC mapping regressions — the final design keeps ABC's natural mapping of `+`/nested muxes where they perform best.

## Final Result

**PASS** — best ADP **1.087e+05** (area 94.7 × delay 1147 ps), achieved by `design.sv` (identical to `design_v14.sv`). Baseline `starting_point.v` was 1.454e+05 → **25% improvement**, bit-exact on all 18.87M vectors.

## 1. Approaches tried

| File | Idea | ADP |
|---|---|---|
| `starting_point.v` | baseline: raw significands, product-side LZC, 3 wide shifters | 145429 |
| `design_v2/v3.sv` | pre-normalize subnormal **inputs** (LZC + 11-bit shift); flat pack; zero-operand exponent trick | 123349 |
| `design_v6.sv` | de-serialized subnormal shifter: select from low sum bits only, sign bit forces zero in parallel | 113515 |
| `design_v7/v8/v10/v11.sv` | raw-significand multiply + window extracts (single / dual, 5/6-bit selects) | 116–124k |
| `design_v12.sv` | direct-form threshold LZC + bit-form overflow | 111506 |
| **`design_v14.sv`** | **+ joint window/sticky extraction** (`{w_ext, prefixOR, 0}` shifted so frac/G/S land at fixed positions) | **108672** |

## 2. Most impactful optimizations
1. **Pre-normalizing subnormal inputs** (v2): product always in [2²⁰,2²²) → no product-side LZC, no left shifter, normal mantissa is a 2:1 mux. Biggest area win.
2. **De-serializing selects from data** (v6): shifter select = `~E_sum[3:0]` (low bits, no carry chain); the slow sign bit (`E_sum ≤ 0`) forces a zero result *in parallel* with the shift instead of gating the select.
3. **Joint window/sticky extraction** (v14): shifting `{w_ext, prefixOR(w_ext), 1'b0}` places fraction, guard, and sticky at fixed bit positions — removed the 25:1 sticky mux (~50 ps + area).
4. Smaller wins: direct-form LZC, bit-form overflow predicate, "zero operands get a hugely negative exponent" (signed zero emerges from the datapath, one less pack mux), flat pack priority (nan > inf > zero > ovf > sub > normal).

## 3. What didn't work (regressions)
- **Raw-product designs (v7/v8/v10/v11)**: moving the shift after the multiplier removes the front-of-path delay but the extractor muxes cost +7–12 area; putting `sub_res` in the extractor *select* path re-serialized adders and was worse still.
- **Custom arithmetic structures**: Kogge-Stone prefix adders (v4/v5), balanced prefix/carry-select incrementers (v9/v17), "flat" per-bit pack formulas (v9) — all regressed; ABC's mapping of plain `+` and nested ternaries was consistently better.
- **Exponent folding** (v15/v16/v19): mathematically cleaner single-adder exponents repeatedly regressed 50–140 ps due to mapping shifts; cy-dependent predicates delayed pack mux selects.
- **target_delay tuning**: 500/1000/1200 give identical netlists (structurally converged); 1800 shrinks area but hurts ADP.

## 4. Lessons / next time
- **ABC is mapping-sensitive**: many "obvious" logic reductions changed the whole netlist and cost 50–150 ps. Always isolate one change per build and keep the previous file (`design_v*.sv`) for revert.
- **Watch what's actually on the critical path** before optimizing: it migrated from shifter-select → multiplier → exponent/predicates as each fix landed.
- Early bugs to check: unsigned-vs-signed compares (`W0 > 7'sd45` with unsigned `W0` broke zero handling), concatenation indexing (`{11'b0, R}` puts R at the bottom), off-by-one in prefix-OR sticky bases.
- Next lever would be the 11×11 multiplier itself (~310 ps, on every path) via an explicit Booth/Dadda tree — untested due to step budget.

================================================================================
